of
g_{n}(w)
and
0\leq j\leq m_{n}(k)-1
. When we treat the case
k=k_{0}
(and necessarily statement
(2)
of Proposition
5.5
), this term is a monomial in
w-w_{k_{0}}
of degree
j<m_{n}(k_{0})
; the statement trivially holds true. So we can assume
k\neq k_{0}
. By a direct computation, we have

\frac{A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}(w-w_{k})^{j}g_{n,\hat{k}}(w)}{g_{n,\hat{k}_{0}}(w)}=A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}(w-w_{k})^{j-m_{n}(k)}(w-w_{k_{0}})^{m_{n}(k_{0})}=\sum_{i\geq m_{n}(k_{0})}a_{k_{0},k,i}^{(j)}(w-w_{k_{0}})^{i},

(5.8.5)

\text{with~}a_{k_{0},k,i}^{(j)}=\binom{j-m_{n}(k)}{i-m_{n}(k_{0})}A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})^{j-m_{n}(k)-i+m_{n}(k_{0})}.

It suffices to prove that
a_{k_{0},k,i}^{(j)}
satisfies the same estimate as
A_{k_{0},i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}
in (
5.5.1
) or (
5.5.3
). We separate the discussion into two cases:

i)

Assume
i=m_{n}(k_{0})
. We first treat statement (2). Using the inequality (
5.2.1
), we can apply Proposition
4.7
(2) and the inequality (
4.9.1
) to the number
A:=A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}
, to deduce

(5.8.6)

v_{p}(A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})})+(j-m_{n}(k))\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})+\Delta_{k_{0},\ell}^{\prime}\geq\Delta_{k_{0},\ell}

with
\ell=n-\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
.
Thus, (
5.8.5
) shows that

\displaystyle v_{p}(a_{k_{0},k,m_{n}(k_{0})}^{(j)})\geq\

\displaystyle v_{p}(A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})})+(j-m_{n}(k))\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:vp(a) geq Delta - Delta'}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{k_{0},\ell}-\Delta^{\prime}_{k_{0},\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:ghost duality}}}{{=}}\Delta_{k_{0},|n-\frac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|}-\Delta^{\prime}_{k_{0},|n-\frac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lemma~\ref{L:useful facts in the proof of Proposition each summand of Lagrange lie above NP}(1)}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{k_{0},\frac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k_{0})}-\Delta_{k_{0},\frac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k_{0})}^{\prime}.

This proves statement (2) of Proposition
5.5
.

The statement (3) can be proved similarly. Using the inequality (
5.2.1
), we can apply Proposition
4.7
(1) to
A:=A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}
, and get the inequality

(5.8.7)

v_{p}(A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})})+jv_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})+v_{p}(g_{n,\hat{k}}(w_{k_{0}}))\geq\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k_{0}},-))_{x=n}.

Combining this with (
5.8.5
) proves Proposition
5.5
(3) in case i) as follows:

v_{p}(a_{k_{0},k,m_{n}(k_{0})}^{(j)})\geq v_{p}(A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})})+j\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:vp(a) geq Np - vp}}}{{\geq}}\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k_{0}},-))_{x=n}-v_{p}(g_{n}(w_{k_{0}})).

ii)

Assume
m_{n}(k_{0})<i<\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}
. We uniformly write the two statements as:

(5.8.8)

v_{p}(a_{k_{0},k,i}^{(j)})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k_{0}))^{2}\big).

Using (
5.8.5
), we deduce that

\displaystyle v_{p}(a_{k_{0},k,i}^{(j)})\geq\

\displaystyle v_{p}(A_{k,j}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})})+(j-m_{n}(k)-i+m_{n}(k_{0}))\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:ghost reduction to k equivalent version}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-j}-\Delta^{\prime}_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)}+(j-m_{n}(k)-i+m_{n}(k_{0}))\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Proposition~\ref{P:Delta - Delta'}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle 1+\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-j)^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k))^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(j-m_{n}(k)-i+m_{n}(k_{0}))\cdot v_{p}(w_{k_{0}}-w_{k}).

Now (
5.8.8
) follows from this and Lemma
5.9
below.

Combining the two cases above, we proved that (
5.8.1
) implies Proposition
5.5
.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
w_{k}
be a ghost zero of
g_{n}(w)
and let
k_{0}=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{0\bullet}
be such that
n<d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
. Fix two integers
i,j
with
m_{n}(k_{0})<i\leq\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}
and
0\leq j\leq m_{n}(k)-1
. Set
\gamma:=v_{p}(k_{\bullet}-k_{0\bullet})
, and

x_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-i,\quad y_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k_{0}),\quad x=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-j,\quad y=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k).

Under these notations, we have the estimate

(5.9.1)

1+\tfrac{1}{2}(x^{2}-y^{2}+y_{0}^{2}-x_{0}^{2})\geq(1+\gamma)(x-y+y_{0}-x_{0}).

Proof.

First note
y_{0}>x_{0}\geq 0
and
x>y\geq 0
under the assumptions in the lemma. We will prove the following equivalent form of (
5.9.1
):

(5.9.2)

(y_{0}-x_{0})(x_{0}+y_{0}-2-2\gamma)+(x-y)(x+y-2-2\gamma)+2\geq 0.

When
\gamma=0
, (
5.9.2
) can be verified directly. If
x_{0}+y_{0}\geq 2\gamma+2
and
x+y\geq 2\gamma+2
, (
5.9.2
) also trivially holds. So we assume
\gamma\geq 1
and either
x_{0}+y_{0}\leq 2\gamma+1
or
x+y\leq 2\gamma+1
from now on.
The rest of the argument takes the form of using
|k_{0\bullet}-k_{\bullet}|\geq p^{\gamma}
to deduce
y_{0}+y\geq O(p^{\gamma})
, and then concluding (
5.9.2
) because either
y
or
y_{0}
is huge. For a rigorous proof, we consider two cases corresponding to Proposition
5.5
(2) and (3) respectively.

(1) Assume that
w_{k_{0}}
is a ghost zero of
g_{n}(w)
. By Lemma
4.10
(1)
, we have

y_{0}+y=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)=|\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n|+|\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n|\geq|\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|=|k_{0\bullet}-k_{\bullet}|\geq p^{\gamma}.

We assume
x+y\leq 2\gamma+1
and the case for
x_{0}+y_{0}\leq 2\gamma+1
can be proved similarly. From
x>y
we have
y\leq\gamma
. Therefore
x_{0}+y_{0}-2-2\gamma\geq y_{0}-2-2\gamma\geq p^{\gamma}-y-2-2\gamma\geq p^{\gamma}-3\gamma-2
and hence
(y_{0}-x_{0})(x_{0}+y_{0}-2-2\gamma)\geq p^{\gamma}-3\gamma-2
. On the other hand, we have
(x-y)(2+2\gamma-x-y)\leq(1+\gamma-y)^{2}\leq(1+\gamma)^{2}
. Combining these two inequalities gives

(y_{0}-x_{0})(x_{0}+y_{0}-2-2\gamma)+(x-y)(x+y-2-2\gamma)+2\geq p^{\gamma}-3\gamma-(1+\gamma)^{2}\geq 0

as
p\geq 7
. This proves (
5.9.2
) or equivalently (
5.9.1
) when
m_{n}(k_{0})>0
.

(2) Assume
d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\geq n
so that
m_{n}(k_{0})=0
and
y_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}
. Since
d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\geq n>d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
, we have
k_{0\bullet}>k_{\bullet}
. By Definition-Proposition
2.13
(5), we have

\displaystyle y_{0}+y=\

\displaystyle(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}-\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})+(d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k))\geq\tfrac{p-1}{p+1}(k_{0\bullet}-k_{\bullet})-2+1\geq\tfrac{p-1}{p+1}\cdot p^{\gamma}-1.

If
x_{0}+y_{0}\leq 2\gamma+1
, we have
y_{0}\leq 2\gamma+1
. Since
k_{0\bullet}>k_{\bullet}
, we have
y_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{new}}>\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)=y
and hence
y\leq 2\gamma
. Then
4\gamma+1\geq y_{0}+y\geq\frac{p-1}{p+1}\cdot p^{\gamma}-1
, which is impossible when
p\geq 11
and
\gamma\geq 1
;

If
x+y\leq 2\gamma+1
, we have
y\leq\gamma
and
y_{0}\geq\frac{p-1}{p+1}\cdot p^{\gamma}-\gamma-1
. Then we have
(y_{0}-x_{0})(x_{0}+y_{0}-2-2\gamma)\geq\frac{p-1}{p+1}\cdot p^{\gamma}-3-3\gamma
and
(x-y)(2+2\gamma-x-y)\leq(1+\gamma)^{2}
. Therefore,

y_{0}^{2}-x_{0}^{2}+x^{2}-y^{2}-2(1+\gamma)(y_{0}-x_{0}+x-y)+2\geq\tfrac{p-1}{p+1}\cdot p^{\gamma}-1-3\gamma-(1+\gamma)^{2}\geq 0

as
p\geq 11
and
\gamma\geq 1
. This completes the proof of (
5.9.1
) when
d_{k_{0}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\geq n
.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to proving the estimate (
5.8.1
). This does not rely on the inductive setup in Proposition
5.5
, and it is a result purely about the matrix of
U_{p}
-operator. Recall the two matrices
\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}
and
Y
defined in Notation
3.18
. For two ordered tuples
\underline{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
,
\underline{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{n}
, write
\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})
for the submatrices with row indices in
\underline{\lambda}
and column indices in
\underline{\eta}
(cf. Notation
3.21
). The first step of the proof is to reduce to an estimate on the determinants of such submatrices.

Proposition 5.10
.

To prove Proposition
5.5
, it suffices to prove the following estimate:

(5.10.1)

v_{p}(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})))\geq\deg g_{n}+\frac{\deg(\underline{\lambda})-\deg(\underline{\eta})}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}!}\Big),

for all subsets
\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
. Here
v_{p}(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})))
denotes the
p
-adic valuation of the determinant in the ring
\mathcal{O}\langle w/p\rangle
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.8
, to prove Proposition
5.5
, it suffices to verify the condition (
5.8.1
).
It follows from Lemma
3.17
and Proposition
3.19
that the product
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}=\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathrm{Y}^{-1}
of infinite matrices converges in
\mathrm{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{O}\langle w/p\rangle)
. By Lemma
A.3
we have

(5.10.2)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\\
\#\underline{\lambda}=\#\underline{\eta}=n\end{subarray}}\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\lambda}))\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{\eta}\times\underline{\xi})).

To prove (
5.8.1
), it suffices to prove that each summand on the right hand side of (
5.10.2
) satisfies the same estimate. We fix two tuples
\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
with
\#\underline{\lambda}=\#\underline{\eta}=n
.

By construction we have
\deg\mathbf{f}_{n}=\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. It follows from Lemma
3.17
that the matrix
\mathrm{Y}
is upper triangular. For
\zeta_{i}\in\underline{\zeta}
and
\lambda_{j}\in\underline{\lambda}
, we have
\mathrm{Y}_{\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}},\mathbf{f}_{\lambda_{j}}}=0
if
\zeta_{i}>\lambda_{j}
. When
\zeta_{i}\leq\lambda_{j}
, by Lemma
3.17
and Lemma
A.5
(2) we have

\displaystyle v_{p}(\mathrm{Y}_{\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}},\mathbf{f}_{\lambda_{j}}})+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}\big)+v_{p}(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}!)

\displaystyle\geq\ \

\displaystyle-v_{p}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}!\big)+\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}}{p}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{p^{2}-p}\Big\rfloor+\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{2}+v_{p}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}!\big)

\displaystyle=\ \

\displaystyle\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{2}+v_{p}\Big(\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}}{p}\Big\rfloor!\Big)-v_{p}\Big(\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor!\Big)-\Big\lfloor\tfrac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}}{p^{2}-p}\Big\rfloor\geq 0.

So we have
v_{p}(\mathrm{Y}_{\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}},\mathbf{f}_{\lambda_{j}}})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\zeta_{i}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}\big)-v_{p}(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{j}}!)
for all
\zeta_{i},\lambda_{j}
’s and hence

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\lambda}))\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\zeta})-\deg(\underline{\lambda}))-\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{p}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}!\big).

By a similar argument we have

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{\eta}\times\underline{\xi}))\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\eta})-\deg(\underline{\xi}))+\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{p}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}!\big).

Combining the above inequalities with (
5.10.1
) we have

(5.10.3)

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\lambda}))\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{\eta}\times\underline{\xi})))\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\deg(\underline{\zeta})-\deg(\underline{\xi})\big)+\deg g_{n},

which proves (
5.8.1
). This completes the proof of Proposition
5.10
.
∎

Write the subsets
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{n}\}
and
\underline{\eta}=\{\eta_{1}<\cdots<\eta_{n}\}
. To be extremely careful about the cases when
a
is close to
1
or
p-1
, we set

(5.10.4)

\boldsymbol{\delta}:=\deg g_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\Big\rfloor\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:degree approx halo bound}}}{{\in}}\{0,1\}.

Moreover,

\boldsymbol{\delta}=1
can happen only when
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}=p-1-a
again by (
2.13.3
).

To prove (
5.10.1
), we make use of the halo estimates near the end of Section 3. We first treat two special cases of (
5.10.1
), which represent different strategies of proofs.

Lemma 5.11
.

The estimate (
5.10.1
) holds in the following two cases:
(1)~

\underline{\lambda}=\underline{n}
and
\underline{\eta}\neq\underline{n}
;
(2)~

\underline{\lambda}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
and
\underline{\eta}=\underline{n}
.

Proof.

(1) By Corollary
3.22
, we have

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{n}\times\underline{\eta})\big)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:bold delta}}}{{=}}\deg g_{n}-\boldsymbol{\delta}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\Big\rfloor\Big).

Comparing this inequality with (
5.10.1
), it suffices to prove the inequality

(5.11.1)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{2}+v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}!}\Big)-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\Big\rfloor\bigg)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}.

By assumption on
\underline{\eta}
, we have
\eta_{i}\geq i
for
i=1,\dots,n-1
and
\eta_{n}\geq n+1
. Therefore
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}\geq\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n-1
and
\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}\geq\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}
as
a\leq p-3
. On the other hand, by Lemma
A.5
(2) we have

\quad v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}!}\Big)-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\Big\rfloor=v_{p}\Big(\frac{\lfloor\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}/p\rfloor!}{\lfloor\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}/p\rfloor!}\Big)\geq 0\text{~for all~}i=1,\dots,n.

Combining these together gives (
5.11.1
), and proves (1). Note that in proving
(1)
we only need
1\leq a\leq p-3
;

(2) Let
\gamma=\max\{v_{p}(i)\,|\,i=\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}+1,\dots,\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}\}
. Since
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}<p
, we have
v_{p}\big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}!}\big)=\gamma
and (
5.10.1
) becomes

(5.11.2)

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\big)\big)\geq\deg g_{n}+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}+\gamma.

By Corollary
3.29
we have

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\big)\big)\geq\mathbb{D}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\Big\rfloor\Big)+\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}\big).

Combining with (
5.10.4
), it suffices to prove the inequality

\mathbb{D}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+\gamma.

Since
\boldsymbol{\delta}=1
only happens when
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}=p-1-a
, the condition
2\leq a\leq p-5
implies that
\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+1
. So we can assume
\gamma\geq 2
and it is enough to prove
\mathbb{D}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\geq\gamma-1
.

Write
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 0}\alpha_{i}p^{i}
and
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 0}\beta_{i}p^{i}
in their
p
-adic expansions. Since
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}<p
and
\gamma\geq 2
, we have
\beta_{0}<\alpha_{0}
,
\alpha_{1}=\cdots=\alpha_{\gamma-1}=p-1
and
\beta_{1}=\cdots=\beta_{\gamma-1}=0
.
By Lemma
A.8
(2)
we have
D_{=0}(\underline{n},0)=\cdots=D_{=0}(\underline{n},\gamma-1)
, so for every
j=1,\dots,\gamma-2
we have

D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)=D_{=0}(\underline{n},j)+1=D_{=0}(\underline{n},j+1)+1,

and hence

\max_{0\leq\alpha\leq p-2}\{D_{\leq\alpha}(\underline{\lambda},j)-D_{\leq\alpha}(\underline{n},j+1),0\}\geq D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)-D_{=0}(\underline{n},j+1)=1

for such
j
’s. For
j=0
, we apply Lemma
A.8
(3)
to
\alpha=\beta_{0}
, and we get

D_{\leq\beta_{0}}(\underline{n},0)=D_{\leq\beta_{0}}(\underline{n},0)+1=D_{\leq\beta_{0}}(\underline{n},1)+1
. Therefore

\max_{0\leq\alpha\leq p-2}\{D_{\leq\alpha}(\underline{\lambda},0)-D_{\leq\alpha}(\underline{n},1),0\}\geq D_{\leq\beta_{0}}(\underline{\lambda},0)-D_{\leq\beta_{0}}(\underline{n},1)=1.

Combining these two inequalities together we have
\mathbb{D}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\geq\gamma-1
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

(1)

The proof of (i) follows from the standard halo estimate in Proposition
3.19
. On the other hand, as shown in the proof of (ii), the usual halo bound in Proposition
3.19
is not enough to control the
\gamma
on the right hand side of (
5.11.2
). The subtle improvement of halo estimate in Corollary
3.29
is essential for this proof.

(2)

When proving Lemma
5.11
(2), it is necessary to use the stronger estimate involving
\mathbb{D}(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})
in (
3.29.1
). However, carefully inspecting the proof, we can ‘almost’ prove the following estimate

(5.12.1)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}-v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}!}\Big)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta},

except the following situation:

\max\{v_{p}(i)\,|\,i=\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}+1,\dots,\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}\}\geq 2
and the last digit of the
p
-adic expansion of
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}
is nonzero. In this situation, we say that the tuple
\underline{\lambda}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is
special
and we only have a weaker estimate

(5.12.2)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}}{2}-v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}!}\Big)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}-1.

We note that from the proof of Lemma
5.11
(2), when
\underline{\lambda}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special, we always have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},0)=D_{=0}(\underline{n},0)=D_{=0}(\underline{n},1)
.

Lemma 5.13
.

The estimate (
5.10.1
) holds if
\underline{\lambda}\neq\underline{n}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.29
, it
suffices to show that

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor\Big)\geq\deg g_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{2}+v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}!}\Big)\bigg),

or equivalently, to show that

(5.13.1)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}+\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{2}+v_{p}\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i}}}{p}\Big\rfloor!\Big)\bigg)\geq\deg g_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{p}(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}!).

by Lemma
A.5
(2). We first reduce the proof of (
5.13.1
) to the case when
\underline{\eta}=\underline{n}
. To do this, it suffices to show that, for a subset

\underline{\eta}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
with
\eta^{\prime}_{i}=\eta_{i}
for all
i
except some
i=i_{0}
for which
\eta^{\prime}_{i_{0}}-\eta_{i_{0}}=1
, we have

(5.13.2)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}^{\prime})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta^{\prime}_{i_{0}}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i_{0}}}}{2}+v_{p}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta^{\prime}_{i_{0}}}/p\rfloor!}{\lfloor\deg\mathbf{e}_{\eta_{i_{0}}}/p\rfloor!}\bigg)\geq D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}).

This inequality follows from Lemma
3.28
.

We assume
\underline{\eta}=\underline{n}
from now on. By Lemma
5.11
(2)
, we need to show that for any subset
\underline{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
with
\underline{\lambda}\neq\underline{n},\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
,

(5.13.3)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{i}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}!}\Big)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}.

Consider the following operation on all subsets
\underline{\lambda}\neq\underline{n},\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
of size
n
: let
n_{-}
be the smallest integer in
\underline{n}\setminus\underline{\lambda}
;
if
\lambda_{n}-n_{-}\geq 2
, we replace
\lambda_{n}
by
n_{-}
to get another subset
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}:=\underline{\lambda}\cup\{n_{-}\}\setminus\{\lambda_{n}\}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
(and properly reorder the elements in this subset).

Claim:

(a) Under such operations, we always have

(5.13.4)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}!}\Big).

(b) Moreover, when
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special (see Remark
5.12
(2)), we have a stronger estimate

(5.13.5)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+v_{p}\Big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}!}\Big)+1.

We first explain that this Claim implies Lemma
5.13
. Indeed, repeatedly apply this operation to
\underline{\lambda}
, we will eventually get
\underline{n}
or
\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
after finite (and at least one) steps. We deduce (
5.13.3
) by separating the following cases. If we get
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\underline{n}
, (
3.30.1
) says that
D(\underline{n},\underline{n})=0
, then

(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n}})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n} for $\underline{\lambda}=\underline{n}$})=\boldsymbol{\delta}.

If we get
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
and it is not special, we get

(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n}})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n} for $\underline{\lambda}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}$})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:`fake' estimate for lambda = 1,dots, n-1,n}}}{{\geq}}\boldsymbol{\delta}.

Finally, if we get
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
and it is special, Claim(b) implies that

(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n}})\geq 1+\boldsymbol{\delta}+(\textrm{L.H.S. of \eqref{E:D lambda n} for $\underline{\lambda}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}$})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:wekaer estimate for lambda = 1,dots, n-1,n}}}{{\geq}}\boldsymbol{\delta}.

We turn to prove the Claim.
Let
\gamma=\max\{v_{p}(i)\,|\,i=\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}+1,\dots,\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}\}
. By Lemma
A.6
we have
v_{p}\big(\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}!}{\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}!}\big)\leq\gamma+\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}-2}{p-1}\rfloor
. So the Claim is reduced to prove

(5.13.6)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}-2}{p-1}\Big\rfloor\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+\gamma

or when
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special

(5.13.7)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})+\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}-2}{p-1}\Big\rfloor\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+\gamma+1.

Let
\delta
be the unique nonnegative integer such that
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}\in((p-1)p^{\delta-1},(p-1)p^{\delta}]
. In particular, we have
\delta=0\Leftrightarrow\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}=p-1\Leftrightarrow\lambda_{n}-n_{-}=2
. Let
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 0}\alpha_{i}p^{i}
and
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 0}\beta_{i}p^{i}
with
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\in\{0,\dots,p-1\}
be their
p
-adic expansions. We divide our discussion into two cases:

Case 1
: Assume
\gamma\leq\delta
. Consider the set
\Omega=\{i\geq 0\,|\,\alpha_{i}\neq 0,\beta_{i}=0\}
. If
\Omega
is nonempty, let
j
be the maximal integer in
\Omega
. If
j\geq\gamma+1
, the integer
m=\sum\limits_{i\geq j}\alpha_{i}p^{i}
lies in the interval
[\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}+1,\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}]
with
v_{p}(m)=j>\gamma
. This contradicts with the definition of
\gamma
. So the cardinal number
\#\Omega
is less or equal to
\gamma+1
and hence

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\geq D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})-\#\Omega\geq D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})-\gamma-1.

To get (
5.13.6
), it suffices to prove the inequality

(5.13.8)

\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}-2}{p-1}\Big\rfloor\geq 2\gamma+\boldsymbol{\delta}+1.

When
\gamma=0
, we must have
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}<p
and hence
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}=p-1
. Then (
5.13.8
) becomes
\frac{p-1}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+1
, which is obvious. When
\gamma=1
, the conditions
2\leq a\leq p-5
and
p\geq 11
implies that the left hand side of (
5.13.8
)
\geq\frac{p-1}{2}\geq 3+\boldsymbol{\delta}
. When
\gamma\geq 2
, from the condition
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}>(p-1)p^{\delta-1}\geq(p-1)p^{\gamma-1}
, we see that the left hand side of (
5.13.8
)
\geq(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p-1})(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}})>\frac{p+1}{2}\cdot p^{\gamma-1}>2\gamma+\boldsymbol{\delta}+1
. This completes the proof of (
5.13.6
) when
\gamma\leq\delta
.

When
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special, we have
n_{-}\leq n<n+1\leq\lambda_{n}
and hence
\gamma\geq\max\{v_{p}(i)\,|\,i=\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}+1,\dots,\deg\mathbf{e}_{n+1}\}\geq 2
. The above discussion actually shows that the left hand side of (
5.13.8
)
\geq 2\gamma+\boldsymbol{\delta}+2
, which gives (
5.13.7
) when
\gamma\leq\delta
.

Case 2
: Assume
\gamma>\delta
. Set
m=\sum\limits_{i\geq\gamma}\alpha_{i}p^{i}
to be the largest integer in
[0,\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}]
with the property
v_{p}(m)\geq\gamma
. By the definition of
\gamma
, we have
m\in[\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}+1,\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}]
and
\alpha_{\gamma}\neq 0
. Then
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-m<\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}\leq(p-1)p^{\delta}
and similarly
m-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}<(p-1)p^{\delta}
. Since
\delta<\gamma
, the
p
-adic expansions of
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}
and
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}
have the following properties:

(a)

\alpha_{\gamma}\neq 0
,
\alpha_{i}=0
for
i=\delta+1,\dots,\gamma-1
;

(b)

\beta_{i}=\alpha_{i}
for
i\geq\gamma+1
,
\beta_{\gamma}=\alpha_{r}-1
,
\beta_{i}=p-1
for
i=\delta+1,\dots,\gamma-1
and
\beta_{\delta}\neq 0
.

Let
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 0}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}p^{i}
be the
p
-adic expansion of
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}
. From
n_{-}\leq n\leq\lambda_{n}
, we have

(c)

\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=\alpha_{i}=\beta_{i}
for all
i>\gamma
.

Based on the two possibilities
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}\in[m,\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}]
or
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}\in[\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}},m)
, exactly one of the following two cases holds:

(d)

\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=0
for all
i=\delta+1,\dots,\gamma-1
;

(e)

\alpha^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}=\alpha_{\gamma}-1
,
\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=p-1
for all
i=\delta+1,\dots,\gamma-1
and
\alpha_{\delta}^{\prime}\geq\beta_{\delta}>0
.

By the definition of
D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})
in Notation
3.25
, we can write

(5.13.9)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})-D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})=\sum_{j\geq 0}D_{j},

with
D_{j}=\max\{D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)-D_{=0}(\underline{n},j+1),0\}-\max\{D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},j)-D_{=0}(\underline{n},j+1),0\}
. We estimate each
D_{j}
as follows:

(i)

When
j\geq\gamma+1
, from
\alpha_{j}=\beta_{j}
we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)=D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},j)
and hence
D_{j}=0
;

(ii)

When
j=\gamma
, it follows from the definitions of
n_{-}
and
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}
that the integers in
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}\setminus\underline{n}
belong to the interval
(n_{-},\lambda_{n})
. From the information on the
p
-adic expansions of
\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}},\deg\mathbf{e}_{n}
and
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}
listed in (a)
-
(e) as above, we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},\gamma)\leq D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\gamma)\leq D_{=0}(\underline{n},\gamma)
. By Lemma
A.8
(1),
D_{=0}(\underline{n},\gamma)\leq D_{=0}(\underline{n},\gamma+1)
. So we have
D_{\gamma}=0
;

(iii)

When
j=\gamma-1
, from (a)(b) we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},\gamma-1)=D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\gamma-1)+1
and hence
D_{\gamma-1}\geq 0
;

(iv)

When
j=\delta+1,\dots,\gamma-2
, from (a)(b) we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)=D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},j)+1
. From (c)(d)(e) and Lemma
A.8
(2) we have
D_{=0}(\underline{n},\delta+1)=\cdots=D_{=0}(\underline{n},\gamma-1)
. By a similar discussion as in (ii), from the facts
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}\setminus\underline{n}\subset\{n_{-}+1,\dots,\lambda_{n}-1\}
and (a)
-
(e) above we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},j)\geq D_{=0}(\underline{n},j)
. In summary, we have
D_{j}=1
in this case;

(v)

When
j=\delta
, we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},\delta)\geq D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\delta)
as
\beta_{\delta}\neq 0
. Therefore
D_{\delta}\geq 0
;

(vi)

When
j=0,\dots,\delta-1
, we have
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda},j)\geq D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},j)-1
and hence
D_{j}\geq-1
for such
j
’s. Note that when
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special, it follows from Remark
5.12
(2) that
D_{=0}(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},0)=D_{=0}(\underline{n},1)
and we have
D_{0}\geq 0
in this case.

From the above discussion and (
5.13.9
) we get

(5.13.10)

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\geq D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+(\gamma-\delta-2)-\delta,

where the term
\gamma-\delta-2
comes from case (iv) and
-\delta
comes from case (vi). When
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}=\{1,\dots,n-1,n+1\}
is special, from the discussion in case (iv) we get

D(\underline{\lambda},\underline{n})\geq D(\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},\underline{n})+(\gamma-\delta-2)-(\delta-1).

So to prove (
5.13.6
) or (
5.13.7
), it suffices to prove

(5.13.11)

\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}}{2}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}-2}{p-1}\Big\rfloor\geq 2\delta+2+\boldsymbol{\delta}.

When
\delta=0
, we have
\lambda_{n}-n_{-}=2
and
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\lambda_{n}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{n_{-}}=p-1
. Then (
5.13.11
) becomes
\frac{p-1}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}+2
, which is obvious. When
\delta=1
, we have that the left hand side of (
5.13.11
)
\geq\frac{p-1}{2}\geq 4+\boldsymbol{\delta}
as
p\geq 11
. When
\delta\geq 2
, the left hand side of (
5.13.11
)
\geq\frac{p+1}{2}\cdot p^{\delta-1}>2\delta+\boldsymbol{\delta}+2
. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.13
.
∎

Lemma 5.14
.

The estimate (
5.10.1
) holds for
\underline{\lambda}=\underline{\eta}=\underline{n}
.

Proof.

By (
5.10.2
) and the fact that
\mathrm{Y}^{-1}
is upper triangular, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{\lambda},\underline{\eta}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\\
\#\underline{\lambda}=\#\underline{\eta}=n\end{subarray}}\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{n}\times\underline{\lambda}))\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{\eta})\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{\eta}\times\underline{n}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\\
\#\underline{\lambda}=n\end{subarray}}\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{n}\times\underline{\lambda}))\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{n})\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{n}\times\underline{n})).

Denote

f(w):=\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))-\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{n}))=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{n}\neq\underline{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\\
\#\underline{\lambda}=n\end{subarray}}\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}(\underline{n}\times\underline{\lambda}))\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{n})\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{Y}^{-1}(\underline{n}\times\underline{n})).

Set
d:=\deg g_{n}
. As (
5.10.1
) has been proved for all
\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{\lambda}\times\underline{n})
’s with
\underline{\lambda}\neq\underline{n}
, it follows from the proof of Proposition
5.10
(in particular, the proof of (
5.10.3
)) that we have
f(w)\in p^{d}\mathcal{O}\langle\frac{w}{p}\rangle
. By Corollary
3.11
, we may write
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))=p^{-d}g_{n}(w)h(w)
with

g_{n}(w)=\sum_{i=0}^{d}p^{i}c_{i}w^{d-i}\quad\textrm{and}\quad h(w)=\sum\limits_{j\geq 0}h_{j}\cdot(\tfrac{w}{p})^{j}\in\mathcal{O}\langle\tfrac{w}{p}\rangle,

where each
c_{i},h_{j}\in\mathcal{O}
, and
c_{0}=1
.

We claim that
v_{p}(h)\geq d
, i.e.
v_{p}(h_{j})\geq d
for each
j
. Suppose the contrary, let
m
be the largest integer for which
v_{p}(h_{m})<d
(such
m
exists as
h(w)\in\mathcal{O}\langle\frac{w}{p}\rangle
). Then the
w^{d+m}
-coefficient of
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))=p^{-d}g_{n}(w)h(w)
is

p^{-d}\sum_{i=0}^{d}p^{i}c_{i}\cdot p^{-(m+i)}h_{m+i}=p^{-d-m}\sum_{i=0}^{d}c_{i}h_{m+i},

which has
p
-adic valuation
-d-m+v_{p}(h_{m})<-m
. On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
3.15
that
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{n}))\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket
, and we see from the equality
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))=\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{n}))+f(w)
that the
p
-adic valuation of the
w^{d+m}
-coefficient of
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))
is greater or equal to
-m
, which is a contradiction.

So the claim holds and
v_{p}(h_{m})\geq d
for all
m
and
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{n}))\in g_{n}(w)\mathcal{O}\langle\frac{w}{p}\rangle\subset p^{\deg g_{n}}\mathcal{O}\langle\frac{w}{p}\rangle
. From this, we deduce
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}_{\mathbf{C}}(\underline{n}))\in p^{\deg g_{n}}\mathcal{O}\langle\frac{w}{p}\rangle
.
∎

Now the estimate (
5.10.1
) in Proposition
5.10
follows from combining Lemmas
5.11
,
5.13
, and
5.14
. This completes the proof of Proposition
5.5
.

Remark 5.15
.

We point out that the proof of this proposition is where the condition
a\notin\{1,p-4\}
and
p\geq 11
are used. The problem is rooted in the number
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\deg g_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}-\big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}{p}\big\rfloor\in\{0,1\}
measuring the error from halo estimate in Corollary
3.29
.

6.
Proof of local ghost conjecture III: cofactor expansions

In this section, we execute Step II as outlined at the beginning of Section
4
. More precisely, for a fixed
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, we assume that Theorem
5.2
holds for all submatrices of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}
of size
\leq n-1
, then we aims to prove that Theorem
5.2
holds for all finite subsets
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
of size
n
.
This would then conclude the proof of Theorem
2.8
.
Even though the inductive proof does not start until §
6.11
, it does not hurt to bare in mind of the inductive thinking. Keep the notations from the previous section, and recall that a relevant character
\varepsilon
is fixed throughout yet suppressed from the notation.

This section is organized as follows. In Lemma
6.2
we give a cofactor expansion formula and use it to express
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}))
as a linear combination of determinants of minors of smaller sizes modulo certain powers of
w-w_{k}
in Lemma
6.4
. In Proposition
6.8
we give an estimate of determinants of minors of sizes smaller than
n
, which relies on earlier estimates in Proposition
5.5
. We start the inductive proof of Theorem
5.2
in §
6.11
. Since the proof is rather technical, we first explain our strategy in several simple cases in §
6.16
. The proof of the general case is initiated in §
6.19
and concluded in §
6.23
by assuming a technical result Proposition
6.20
, whose lengthy proof is postponed to §
6.24
till the end of the section.

Notation 6.1
.

In this section, we fix an integer
n\geq 2
and a weight
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
such that
m_{n}(k)\neq 0
.
For subsets
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
, and write
r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
,
s_{\underline{\xi}}
, and
m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
for the integers
r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}(k)
,
s_{\underline{\xi}}(k)
, and
m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}(k)
defined in Definition-Proposition
3.23
, respectively.

Similar to Proposition
3.6
(2), let
\mathrm{L}_{k}\in\mathrm{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{O})
denote the following infinite matrix:

•

the upper-left
(d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\times d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})
-block of
\mathrm{L}_{k}
is the Atkin–Lehner operator
-\mathrm{AL}_{(k,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})}
acting on the power basis
\mathbf{B}_{k}
; it is an antidiagonal matrix whose
(i,d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+1-i)
-entry is
-p^{\deg\mathbf{e}_{i}}
, and

•

entries of
\mathrm{L}_{k}
away from the upper-left
(d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\times d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})
-block are the same as the corresponding entries of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}|_{w=w_{k}}
.

This matrix
\mathrm{L}_{k}
is block upper triangular by (
2.12.2
) of Proposition
2.12
(1).
Then the difference
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}|_{w=w_{k}}-\mathrm{L}_{k}
has rank at most
d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

We also need a sign convention: when computing the determinant of a matrix like
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})
, we write the row and columns in increasing order of the numbers in
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
. For a subset
I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}
, we write
\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})
to mean the sign of permutation that sends
\underline{\zeta}
to the
ordered
disjoint union of
I\sqcup(\underline{\zeta}-I)
, where elements in each of
I
and
\underline{\zeta}-I
are in increasing order.

The following key linear algebra result roughly states that, modulo an appropriate power of
w-w_{k}
, we may express the determinant of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})
as the linear combination of determinants of minors of smaller sizes.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
k
,
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}
,
\mathrm{L}_{k}
,
\underline{\zeta}
, and
\underline{\xi}
be as above.
Fix a subset
J_{0}\subseteq\underline{\xi}
. We write

\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0}):=\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})-\mathrm{L}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times J_{0})\in\mathrm{M}_{n\times n}(\mathcal{O}\langle w/p\rangle),

where we view
\mathrm{L}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times J_{0})
as a matrix indexed by
\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}
by filling the remaining entries in the
\underline{\zeta}\times(\underline{\xi}-J_{0})
-submatrix by
0
. Then

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})\big)=\sum_{J\subseteq J_{0}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=\#J\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\#J}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big).

In particular, as power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
, we have the following congruence

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times

\displaystyle\underline{\xi})\big)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J_{0}\\
J\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=\#J\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\#J-1}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big)\qquad\bmod(w-w_{k})^{\corank\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})|_{w=w_{k}}}.

Proof.

By the formula of the determinant of the sum of two matrices (Lemma
A.2
), we get

\mathrm{det}\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})=\sum_{J\subset\underline{\xi}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=\#J\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\cdot\mathrm{det}(-\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times(J\cap J_{0})))\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big).

But the term in the sum is zero when
J\not\subseteq J_{0}
. So the formula simplifies to (
6.2.1
) (after taking out the signs on the entries of
\mathrm{L}_{k}
).
For example, if
\mathrm{L}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})
has only four nonzero entries, at the (upper left)

\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}\times\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\}
-minor, and
J_{0}=\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\}
, then the formula
(
6.2.1
) reads

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})\big)=\

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)-\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}(-1)^{i-j}L_{\zeta_{i},\xi_{j}}\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-\zeta_{i})\times(\underline{\xi}-\xi_{j})\big)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{det}{\begin{pmatrix}L_{\zeta_{1},\xi_{1}}&L_{\zeta_{1},\xi_{2}}\\
L_{\zeta_{2},\xi_{1}}&L_{\zeta_{2},\xi_{2}}\end{pmatrix}}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\})\times(\underline{\xi}-\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\})\big),

where
L_{\zeta_{i},\xi_{j}}
is the
(\zeta_{i},\xi_{j})
-entry of
\mathrm{L}_{k}
.

Now, by Lemma
3.10
,
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})\big)
is divisible by

(w-w_{k})^{\corank\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})|_{w=w_{k}}}
in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
. So the congruence relation (
6.2.2
) follows
immediately from this and (
6.2.1
).
∎

Notation 6.3
.

For the
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
above, let
J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
denote the set consisting of
all

\xi_{j}\in\underline{\xi}
such that either
\xi_{j}>d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
or
d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+1-\xi_{j}\in\underline{\zeta}
. Then
\#J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
.
The following notation reorganizes the congruence relation from Lemma
6.2
: for every
j\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
, denote

(6.3.1)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J=j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big).

This is a signed sum of the products of the determinants of some minors of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}
of size
n-j
, with the determinants of the complement minors in
\mathrm{L}_{k}
.
In particular,
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{0}=\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
. Applying Lemma
6.2
above to the case
J_{0}=J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
, we deduce that

(6.3.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}

\displaystyle\times\underline{\xi})\big)\equiv\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{1}-\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{2}+\cdots

\displaystyle+(-1)^{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1}\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}\qquad\bmod(w-w_{k})^{n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Note that from the proof of Definition-Proposition
3.23
,
\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}})\big|_{w=w_{k}}
has corank at least
n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Our argument needs a more elaborated version of (
6.3.2
), with one goal: we try to write
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
as a linear combination of minors of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}
of smallest possible size (after modulo an appropriate power of
w-w_{k}
). More precisely, we have the following:

Lemma 6.4
.

Keep the notation as above. For a fixed nonnegative integer
j_{0}\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, we have the following congruence of power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

(6.4.1)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)\equiv\sum_{j=j_{0}+1}^{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\binom{j-1}{j_{0}}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}\quad\bmod(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}.

More generally, for every pair of non-negative integers
\ell
and
j_{0}
such that
\ell\leq j_{0}\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, we have the following congruence of power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

(6.4.2)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}\equiv\sum_{j=j_{0}+1}^{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell}\binom{j}{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}\ \bmod(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}.

Remark 6.5
.

We point out that (
6.4.2
) is especially powerful when
n\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
; in this case, we may take
j_{0}
to be
2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
yet still get all information modulo
(w-w_{k})^{m_{n}(k)}
. In other words, we may detect
\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}))\bmod(w-w_{k})^{m_{n}(k)}
using minors of size
\leq 2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
.

Proof.

The congruence (
6.4.1
) is a special case of (
6.4.2
) when setting
\ell=0
.
We first prove (
6.4.2
) in the special case when
\ell=j_{0}
. When
\ell=j_{0}=0
, this is exactly (
6.3.2
).
To treat the general case with
\ell=j_{0}
, we apply Lemma
6.2
(especially (
6.2.2
)) to each factor
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big)
appearing in (
6.3.1
), to deduce the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J^{\prime}\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-J\\
J^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\subseteq\underline{\zeta}-I\\
\#I^{\prime}=\#J^{\prime}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\#J^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{sgn}(I^{\prime},\underline{\zeta}-I)\mathrm{sgn}(J^{\prime},\underline{\xi}-J)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I^{\prime}\times J^{\prime})\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I-I^{\prime})\times(\underline{\xi}-J-J^{\prime}))\big)

modulo
(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}
. Here we used
Definition-Proposition
3.23
to deduce that
\rank\,\mathrm{T}_{k}\big((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J);J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-J\big)|_{w=w_{k}}
is at most
d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
and so its corank is at least

n-j_{0}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Set
I^{\prime\prime}=I\sqcup I^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}=J\sqcup J^{\prime}
, both written in increasing order following Notation
6.1
. Put
j:=\#I^{\prime\prime}=\#J^{\prime\prime}>j_{0}
. The above long expression for
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}}
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{j>j_{0}}

\displaystyle(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J^{\prime\prime}\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq I^{\prime\prime}\\
\#I=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J^{\prime\prime}\\
\#J=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\mathrm{sgn}(I^{\prime\prime}-I,\underline{\zeta}-I)\mathrm{sgn}(J^{\prime\prime}-J,\underline{\xi}-J)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}((I^{\prime\prime}-I)\times(J^{\prime\prime}-J))\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I^{\prime\prime})\times(\underline{\xi}-J^{\prime\prime}))\big).

Applying the sign equality in Lemma
A.2
(1) to
I^{\prime\prime}=I\sqcup I^{\prime}
and to
J^{\prime\prime}=J\sqcup J^{\prime}
, we may rewrite the above sum as

\displaystyle\sum_{j>j_{0}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J^{\prime\prime}\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I^{\prime\prime},\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J^{\prime\prime},\underline{\xi})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I^{\prime\prime})\times(\underline{\xi}-J^{\prime\prime}))\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq I^{\prime\prime}\\
\#I=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J^{\prime\prime}\\
\#J=j_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,I^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{sgn}(J,J^{\prime\prime})\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}((I^{\prime\prime}-I)\times(J^{\prime\prime}-J))\big).

Applying Lemma
A.2
(2) to the second row of the above formula, it is equal to

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}}

\displaystyle\ \equiv\sum_{j>j_{0}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J^{\prime\prime}\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J^{\prime\prime}=j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I^{\prime\prime},\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J^{\prime\prime},\underline{\xi})\cdot

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I^{\prime\prime})\times(\underline{\xi}-J^{\prime\prime}))\big)\cdot\binom{j}{j_{0}}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I^{\prime\prime}\times J^{\prime\prime})\big)

modulo
(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}
.
This is exactly (
6.4.2
) when
\ell=j_{0}
.

We now prove (
6.4.2
) in general by induction on the difference
j_{0}-\ell
. The base case when
\ell=j_{0}
is just treated. Assume that we have proved (
6.4.2
) with smaller
j_{0}-\ell
. Then we have the following congruences (corresponding to the cases of
(\ell,j_{0}-1)
and
(j_{0},j_{0})
).

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}

\displaystyle\,\equiv\sum_{j>j_{0}-1}(-1)^{j-j_{0}}\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell-1}\binom{j}{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}\ \bmod(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}+1\}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}}

\displaystyle\,\equiv\sum_{j>j_{0}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\binom{j}{j_{0}}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}\hskip 9.24994pt\bmod(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}.

Plugging the second congruence into the first one (and modulo the smaller power
(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}
), we immediate deduce (
6.4.2
) by noting that

\binom{j_{0}}{\ell}\binom{j}{j_{0}}-\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell-1}\binom{j}{\ell}=\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell}\binom{j}{\ell}.\qed

Remark 6.6
.

We point out a variant of the above lemma that we will use later. Fix any power series
\eta(w)\in 1+(w-w_{k})E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
.
For
J_{0}\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
, write

\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0}):=\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})-\eta(w)^{-1}\cdot\mathrm{L}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times J_{0})\in\mathrm{M}_{\infty}(E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket);

then we obtain a formula of
\mathrm{det}\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})\big)
analogous to (
6.2.1
), with additional factor
\eta(w)^{-\#J}
on the right hand side. Yet
\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})|_{w=w_{k}}=\mathrm{T}_{k}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi};J_{0})|_{w=w_{k}}
have the same corank.
So if we define the analogue of (
6.3.1
) to be

(6.6.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)^{\sim}_{j}:=\eta(w)^{-j}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}\\
\#I=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}\\
\#J=j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{sgn}(I,\underline{\zeta})\mathrm{sgn}(J,\underline{\xi})\cdot\eta(w)^{-j}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big),

exactly the same argument in Lemmas
6.2
and
6.4
shows that,
for every nonnegative integers
\ell\leq j_{0}\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, we have the following congruence of power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

(6.6.2)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}^{\sim}\equiv\sum_{j>j_{0}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell}\binom{j}{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}^{\sim}\ \bmod(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}.

Notation 6.7
.

To further simplify notations later, we normalize

(6.7.1)

B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}:=A_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}\cdot g_{n,\hat{k}}(w_{k}).

By Lemma
4.10
(1)
, condition (
5.2.1
) is equivalent to, for
i=0,1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1
,

(6.7.2)

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n).

Further,
we normalize the minors appearing in the formula (
6.4.2
) as follows and consider their expansions as power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

(6.7.3)

p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi})-\deg(\underline{\zeta}))}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}}{g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w)/g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k})}=\sum_{i\geq 0}B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}(w-w_{k})^{i}.

This normalization has in mind that the natural way to understand each sum of minor determinants appearing in
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}
is through its Lagrange interpolation along
g_{n-\ell}(w)
.
In particular for
\ell=0
, by comparing (
5.1.1
) and (
6.7.3
), we see that
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},0)}
is equal to
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}
in (
6.7.1
) for
i=0,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1
.

As a convention, if
i<0
, we set
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}=0
.

The following estimate on
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}
can be harvested from the inductive hypothesis and Proposition
5.5
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Assume that
p\geq 11
and
2\leq a\leq p-5
.
Keep the notation as above and assume that Theorem
5.2
holds for all minors of size strictly smaller than
n
.

(1)

Suppose that
\ell
is a positive integer such that
\ell\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
and that
1\leq m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
. (In particular,
\ell<n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.) Then for every
i\in\{m_{n-\ell}(k),\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
,

(6.8.1)

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}\big)\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k)}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k))^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big)\hskip-10.0pt

(6.8.2)

\displaystyle\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

(2)

Suppose that
\ell
is a positive integer such that
\ell\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
and that
m_{n-\ell}(k)=0
. (This implies that
\ell\geq n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.)
Then for every
i\in\{m_{n-\ell}(k),\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
,

(6.8.3)

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}\big)\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big)

(6.8.4)

\displaystyle\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

Later, we will refer (
6.8.1
) and (
6.8.3
) as the
strong estimates
and refer (
6.8.2
) and (
6.8.4
) as the
weak estimates
.

Remark 6.9
.

When
n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, the condition
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
is automatic as long as
\ell\geq 1
, but when
n\geq\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, the condition
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
is equivalent to requiring
\ell\geq 2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+1
. We will use this equivalent condition in later argument.

Proof.

(
6.8.2
) (resp. (
6.8.4
)) follows from (
6.8.1
) (resp. (
6.8.3
)) and Proposition
2.22
. So it suffices to prove (
6.8.1
) and (
6.8.3
). Since we assume that Theorem
5.2
holds for minors of size strictly smaller than
n
, we can apply Proposition
5.5
to such minors.

By (
6.3.1
),
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}

is a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear combination of the terms
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))
over subsets
I\subseteq\underline{\zeta}
and
J\subseteq J_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
of cardinality
\ell
. Fix such two subsets
I
and
J
. Consider the following formal expansion in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

(6.9.1)

p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi})-\deg(\underline{\zeta}))}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)\big)\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))}{g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w)/g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k})}=\sum_{i\geq 0}B^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},I,J)}_{k,i}(w-w_{k})^{i}.

Here we use Notation
5.7
to treat case (1) and (2) uniformly. Under Notation
5.1
, we have a formal expansion in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi}-J)-\deg(\underline{\zeta}-I))}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))\big)}{g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w)}=\sum_{i\geq 0}A_{k,i}^{((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))}(w-w_{k})^{i}.

Comparing this with (
6.9.1
), we deduce that

(6.9.2)

B^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},I,J)}_{k,i}=p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(J)-\deg(I))}\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J))\cdot g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k})\cdot A_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J)}.

To prove the inequality (
6.8.1
) or (
6.8.3
), it suffices to prove the corresponding estimates for
v_{p}\big(B^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},I,J)}_{k,i}\big)
, that is, to prove the inequality

(6.9.3)

v_{p}\big(B^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},I,J)}_{k,i}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k)}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k))^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big).

First we give an estimate of
v_{p}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J))
:

(6.9.4)

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J))\big)\geq\ \tfrac{k-2}{2}\cdot\ell+\tfrac{1}{2}(\deg(I)-\deg(J)).

Write
J=J^{\prime}\sqcup J^{\prime\prime}
with
J^{\prime}=J\cap\underline{d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}}
. For each
\xi\in J^{\prime}
, write
\xi^{\mathrm{op}}:=d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+1-\xi\in\underline{\zeta}
(since
\xi\in J_{\underline{\zeta},\underline{\xi}}
).
Define
I^{\prime}:=\{\xi^{\mathrm{op}}\,|\,\xi\in J^{\prime}\}
and
I^{\prime\prime}=I\backslash I^{\prime}
.
Then the
\xi
th column of
\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)
has only one nonzero entry at
(\xi^{\mathrm{op}},\xi)
, which is
-p^{\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi^{\mathrm{op}}}}
as introduced in Notation
6.1
. So

\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J))=\pm p^{\sum_{\xi\in J^{\prime}}\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi^{\mathrm{op}}}}\cdot\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I^{\prime\prime}\times J^{\prime\prime})).

Taking into account of the equality
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi^{\mathrm{op}}}=k-2-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi}=\tfrac{k-2}{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi^{\mathrm{op}}}-\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi}\big)
by Proposition
2.12
(2), we see that (
6.9.4
) is equivalent to the following

(6.9.5)

v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I^{\prime\prime}\times J^{\prime\prime}))\big)\geq\tfrac{k-2}{2}\cdot\#J^{\prime\prime}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\deg(I^{\prime\prime})-\deg(J^{\prime\prime})).

As every element
\xi\in J^{\prime\prime}
satisfies
\deg\mathbf{e}_{\xi}>k-2
and thus
\frac{k-2}{2}\#J^{\prime\prime}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg(J^{\prime\prime})
, it suffices to prove
v_{p}\big(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I^{\prime\prime}\times J^{\prime\prime}))\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg(I^{\prime\prime})
. But this is because the
\zeta
’s row of
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}|_{w=w_{k}}
belongs to
p^{\deg(\mathbf{e}_{\zeta})}\mathcal{O}
by Proposition
3.2
(2) Now we proved the estimate (
6.9.4
) of
v_{p}(\mathrm{det}(\mathrm{L}_{k}(I\times J)))
.

In view of the equality (
6.9.2
) and the estimate (
6.9.4
), to prove (
6.9.3
), it suffices to prove

(6.9.6)

\displaystyle v_{p}(\,

\displaystyle A_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J)})\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}+m_{n-\ell}(k)}-v_{p}(g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k}))-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\cdot\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n+\ell\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k))^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big).

We separate the discussion for (1) and (2) of the proposition.

(1)

Under the assumption
1\leq m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq i\leq m_{n}(k)-1
in
(1)
, we can apply Proposition
5.5
(2) to the ghost zero
w_{k}
of
g_{n-\ell}(w)
and get

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(A_{k,i}^{((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))}\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k))^{2}\big)+\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k)}-\Delta^{\prime}_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k)}.

Then (
6.9.6
) follows from this and the following equality (from the definition of
\underline{\Delta}_{k}
):

v_{p}\big(g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k})\big)-\tfrac{k-2}{2}(n-\ell-\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:definition of Delta'}}}{{=}}\Delta^{\prime}_{k,n-\ell-\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Lemma~\ref{L:useful facts in the proof of Proposition each summand of Lagrange lie above NP}(1)}}{{=}}\Delta^{\prime}_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-\ell}(k)}.

(2)

Under the assumption
m_{n-\ell}(k)=0
of
(2)
, similarly apply Proposition
5.5
(3) to
w_{k}
and the subsets
\underline{\zeta}-I
,
\underline{\xi}-J
gives the estimate

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(A_{k,i}^{((\underline{\zeta}-I)\times(\underline{\xi}-J))}\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})^{2}\big)+\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-))_{x=n-\ell}-v_{p}\big(g_{n-\ell}(w_{k})\big),

Thus, for (
6.9.6
), it suffices to prove

\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-))_{x=n-\ell}\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\cdot\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n+\ell\big).

But this follows from
[
LTXZ

24

, Proposition 4.28]
and the definition of
\underline{\Delta}_{k}
:

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(g_{d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}}(w_{k})\big)-\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-))_{x=n-\ell}\leq\

\displaystyle\tfrac{k-2}{p+1}(d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-n+\ell)\leq\tfrac{k-2}{2}(d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-n+\ell),

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}=\Delta^{\prime}_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}=\

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(g_{d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}}(w_{k})\big)+\tfrac{k-2}{2}\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}.

We have now completed the proof of (
6.9.6
) and the proposition.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

We cannot upgrade the weak estimates in Proposition
6.8
to

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}\big)\geq\Delta^{\prime}_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n),

because we made use of Proposition
5.5
(2)(3) in the proof; see Remark
5.6
(2) for further discussions.

6.11.
Proof of Theorem
5.2

We are now ready to start the proof of Theorem
5.2
, by induction on
n
. The case of
n=1
has been handled in §
5.4
.

Assumption 6.12
.

For the rest of this section, we assume that Theorem
5.2
holds for all
k
and all subsets
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size strictly smaller than the fixed integer
n
.

We will prove Theorem
5.2
for all
n\times n
minors.
Now we fix an integer
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
such that
m_{n}(k)\neq 0
, and two finite subsets
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
of cardinality
n
.

Consider the elements
B^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})}_{k,i}
for
i=1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1
defined in Notation
6.7
by the Lagrange interpolation of
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
along
g_{n}(w)
(after an appropriate normalization), or equivalently determined by the Taylor expansion of
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
as a power series in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
. We will prove inductively the following.

Theorem 6.13
.

Keep Assumption
6.12
, and for two subsets
\underline{\zeta}
and
\underline{\xi}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
of size
n
, define
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}
as in Notation
6.7
.
Then for every
i\leq m_{n}(k)-1
and every
\ell\in\big\{0,1,\dots,r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}\big\}
,
such that
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)
,
we have

(6.13.1)

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

Then condition (
6.7.2
) or equivalently Theorem
5.2
is the special case of Theorem
6.13
when
\ell=0
.

Remark 6.14
.

Similar to Remark
6.9
, we point out that when
n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
,
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)
is automatic, yet when
n\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, the condition
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)
is equivalent to either
\ell=0
or
\ell\geq 2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
. Moreover, if
\ell\geq 2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, we always have
m_{n-\ell}(k)=n-\ell-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Notation 6.15
.

(1)

For the rest of this section, we will not work with a specific minor of
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
but only with the terms
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}
’s for
0\leq\ell\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
defined in (
6.3.1
). Therefore we shall keep the notation
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}
defined in (
6.7.3
) in the statement of various theorems, propositions and lemmas below but remove the term
\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}
from
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}
in the proofs, by writing
B_{k,i}^{(\ell)}
instead;

(2)

For every positive integer
d
, we set

\tilde{g}_{d}(w):=g_{d,\hat{k}}(w)/g_{d,\hat{k}}(w_{k}).

Note that this notation is meaningful even if
m_{d}(k)=0
(see Notation
5.7
).

6.16.
First stab at Theorem
6.13

Definition-Proposition
3.23
says that
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
and more generally every
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}
is divisible by
(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}\}}
in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
. So
if
i<m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}
,
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell)}=0
and the corresponding condition (
6.13.1
) automatically holds.

Now consider the next easiest case when
i=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}
. We may assume that
i\geq 0
, otherwise there is nothing to prove. Since
i\leq m_{n}(k)-1<\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}
, we have
n-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}=d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}+i<\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
and hence
m_{n-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}}(k)=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=i
.
So in the particular case when
\ell=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
, the weak estimate (
6.8.2
) or (
6.8.4
) (depending on whether
m_{n-\ell}(k)=0
or not) exactly gives (
6.13.1
).

Now we assume that
\ell\in\{0,\dots,r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1\}
.
Applying Lemma
6.4
to the case when
j_{0}=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, we deduce that

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}\equiv\binom{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}\quad\bmod(w-w_{k})^{i+1}.

Note that by Definition-Proposition
3.23
, both sides of the above equality are divisible by
(w-w_{k})^{m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}}=(w-w_{k})^{i}
.
Comparing the coefficients of
(w-w_{k})^{i}
, we immediately get

(6.16.1)

B_{k,i}^{(\ell)}=\binom{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}{\ell}B_{k,i}^{(r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}})},\ \textrm{and thus}

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(\ell)}\big)=v_{p}\Big(\binom{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}{\ell}B_{k,i}^{(r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}})}\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:estimate of smaller minors weak}\textrm{ or }\eqref{E:estimate of smaller minors weak when m n-ell (k)=0}}}{{\geq}}\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

This proves Theorem
6.13
when
i=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}
.

Since the situation in general is more complicated, we consider another case when
i=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+1=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}+1
, to illustrate the new phenomenon. First of all, in the special cases
\ell=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
and
\ell=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, Theorem
6.13
just restates the weak estimate (
6.8.2
) or (
6.8.4
).
So we assume below that
\ell\in\{0,\dots,r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-2\}
. We apply Lemma
6.4
to the case when
j_{0}=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-2
to deduce that, modulo
(w-w_{k})^{i+1}
,

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}\equiv\binom{j_{0}+1}{\ell}\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}+1}-(j_{0}-\ell+1)\binom{j_{0}+2}{\ell}\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j_{0}+2}.

Dividing both sides by
p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi})-\deg(\underline{\zeta}))}\cdot\tilde{g}_{n-\ell}(w)=p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi})-\deg(\underline{\zeta}))}\cdot g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w)/g_{n-\ell,\hat{k}}(w_{k})
and further by
(w-w_{k})^{i-1}
(to kill the auxiliary powers), we arrive at, modulo
(w-w_{k})^{2}
,

(6.16.2)

\displaystyle B^{(\ell)}_{k,i-1}+B^{(\ell)}_{k,i}(w-w_{k})\equiv\ \binom{j_{0}+1}{\ell}\frac{\tilde{g}_{n-j_{0}-1}(w)}{\tilde{g}_{n-\ell}(w)}\Big(B^{(j_{0}+1)}_{k,i-1}+B^{(j_{0}+1)}_{k,i}(w-w_{k})\Big)\qquad

\displaystyle-(j_{0}-\ell+1)\binom{j_{0}+2}{\ell}\frac{\tilde{g}_{n-j_{0}-2}(w)}{\tilde{g}_{n-\ell}(w)}\Big(B^{(j_{0}+2)}_{k,i-1}+B^{(j_{0}+2)}_{k,i}(w-w_{k})\Big).

Here recall that
\tilde{g}_{d}(w)
was introduced in Notation
6.15
(2).

Suggested by this, we consider the following.

Notation 6.17
.

For every
j\geq 0
, we write the following power series expansion:

(6.17.1)

\eta_{j}(w):=\frac{\tilde{g}_{n-j}(w)}{\tilde{g}_{n}(w)}=1+\eta_{j,1}(w-w_{k})+\eta_{j,2}(w-w_{k})^{2}+\cdots\in E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket.

Comparing the
(w-w_{k})
-coefficients in (
6.16.2
), we deduce

\displaystyle B_{k,i}^{(\ell)}\

\displaystyle=\binom{j_{0}+1}{\ell}B_{k,i}^{(j_{0}+1)}-(j_{0}-\ell+1)\binom{j_{0}+2}{\ell}B_{k,i}^{(j_{0}+2)}

\displaystyle+\;\binom{j_{0}+1}{\ell}(\eta_{j_{0}+1,1}-\eta_{\ell,1})B_{k,i-1}^{(j_{0}+1)}-(j_{0}-\ell+1)\binom{j_{0}+2}{\ell}(\eta_{j_{0}+2,1}-\eta_{\ell,1})B_{k,i-1}^{(j_{0}+2)}.

By the weak estimate (
6.8.2
) or (
6.8.4
), the first two terms above have
p
-adic valuation greater than or equal to
\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\frac{k-2}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)
. But we need to show the sum of the latter two terms does not interfere here. Our strategy is to show that
the power series
\eta_{j}(w)
is “approximately” the same as
\eta_{1}(w)^{j}
, and thus each
\eta_{j,1}
is “approximately” equal to
j\cdot\eta_{1,1}
, and thus we are reduced to prove

(6.17.2)

\binom{j_{0}+1}{\ell}\cdot(j_{0}-\ell+1)\cdot B_{k,i-1}^{(j_{0}+1)}=(j_{0}-\ell+2)(j_{0}-\ell+1)\binom{j_{0}+2}{\ell}\cdot B_{k,i-1}^{(j_{0}+2)},

which follows from what we just proved in the case of
i=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}(k)
, namely (
6.16.1
).

Remark 6.18
.

It is important to cancel the major terms in different
\eta
-functions, especially when
i
is almost as large as
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}
; in this case, the difference
\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(i-1)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}\approx\frac{p-1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)
, yet the term
\eta_{\ell,1}
roughly has
p
-adic valuation equal to the maximal
v_{p}(w_{k^{\prime}}-w_{k})
, for all
k^{\prime}
running over the zeros of
g_{n}(w)
, which is about
\ln k/\ln p
. We will show below that the terms that do not get canceled through (
6.17.2
) have relatively large
p
-adic valuation, controlled by the difference
\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(i-1)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}
.

Implementing this strategy in the special case is not particularly easier than the general case. So we now proceed directly to prove Theorem
6.13
(in the general case).

6.19.
Proof of Theorem
6.13

The proof is
by induction on
i
, starting with the smallest case
i=m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}-s_{\underline{\xi}}
already treated in §
6.16
(and when
i<m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}
, Theorem
6.13
also holds automatically.)
Now, let
i_{0}\in\{m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
, and suppose that Theorem
6.13
has been proved for all nonnegative integers
i<i_{0}
. We may clearly assume that
i_{0}\geq 0
, as otherwise there is nothing to prove.
We set

j_{0}:=r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-(i_{0}-m_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+1)=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-i_{0}-1.

The meaning of
j_{0}
is that we will reduce to minors of size at least
j_{0}
smaller than
\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})
. We point out that,

(1)

when
n\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
,
i<m_{n}(k)=d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-n
; so we have
j_{0}\geq n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-(d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-n)=2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
;

(2)

when
n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, similar estimate only shows that
j_{0}\geq 0
.

When
\ell>j_{0}
, we have
