n-\ell\leq d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}+i_{0}
. Then we get
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq i_{0}<m_{n}(k)
and thus Theorem
6.13
just repeats the weak estimate (
6.8.2
) or (
6.8.4
).

We henceforth assume
\ell\in\{0,\dots,j_{0}\}
and still require
m_{n-\ell}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)
.
First, we apply Lemma
6.4
to deduce that

(6.19.1)

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell}\equiv\sum_{j>j_{0}}(-1)^{j-j_{0}-1}\binom{j-\ell-1}{j_{0}-\ell}\binom{j}{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}\quad\bmod(w-w_{k})^{i_{0}+1}.

As explained above, the condition
j>j_{0}
implies that
m_{n-j}(k)<m_{n}(k)
. So Proposition
6.8
applies to this situation and gives estimates of
\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}
. Since (
6.19.1
) involves minors of
\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)
of different sizes, instead of using the the numbers
B_{k,i}^{(j)}
’s to express the Taylor expansion of above in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
, we define the following:

(6.19.2)

\Big(\sum_{i\geq 0}B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}(w-w_{k})^{i}\Big)\cdot\frac{\eta_{j}(w)}{\eta_{1}(w)^{j}}=\sum_{i\geq 0}C_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}(w-w_{k})^{i}\ \in\ E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket.

Or equivalently by (
6.7.3
), in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
, we have an equality

(6.19.3)

p^{\frac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\xi})-\deg(\underline{\zeta}))}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j}}{\tilde{g}_{n}(w)}\cdot\eta_{1}(w)^{-j}=\sum_{i\geq 0}C_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}(w-w_{k})^{i}.

In the following, we adopt similar convention for
C_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}
’s as that for
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}
’s in Notation
6.15
.

In fact, changing from
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}
to
C_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}
is “harmless” for the purpose of our proof.

Proposition 6.20
.

Fix a nonnegative integer
i_{0}\leq m_{n}(k)-1
and
j\in\{0,\dots,r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}\}
such that
m_{n-j}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)
.
Assume that (
6.13.1
) holds for all
B_{k,i}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}
with
0\leq i<i_{0}
. Then

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i_{0}}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)

\Longleftrightarrow\quad v_{p}\big(C_{k,i_{0}}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

We temporarily assume this technical result, whose proof will be given later in §
6.24
.

Remark 6.21
.

For the rest of the inductive proof of Theorem
6.13
, we will only need the analogue of the weaker version of Proposition
6.8
:
v_{p}\big(C_{k,i}^{(\ell)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)
when
i\geq m_{n-j}(k)
. The stronger estimates in Proposition
6.8
are only used to enable transferring estimates between
B_{k,i}^{(\ell)}
’s and
C_{k,i}^{(\ell)}
’s (which is wrapped up in Proposition
6.20
).

Lemma 6.22
.

For every nonnegative integer
\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}_{0}\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
,
we have

(6.22.1)

C_{k,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j^{\prime}_{0}-1}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},\ell^{\prime})}=\sum_{j^{\prime}=j^{\prime}_{0}+1}^{r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}}(-1)^{j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}_{0}-1}\binom{j^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime}-1}{j^{\prime}_{0}-\ell^{\prime}}\binom{j^{\prime}}{\ell^{\prime}}C_{k,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j^{\prime}_{0}-1}^{(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi},j^{\prime})}

Proof.

Applying Remark
6.6
to the case
\eta(w)=\eta_{1}(w)
, then
(
6.6.2
) implies that for every nonnegative integer
\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}_{0}\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}-1
, modulo
(w-w_{k})^{\max\{0,n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j_{0}\}}
in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
,

\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{\ell^{\prime}}\cdot\eta_{1}(w)^{-\ell^{\prime}}\equiv\sum_{j^{\prime}>j^{\prime}_{0}}(-1)^{j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}_{0}-1}\binom{j^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime}-1}{j^{\prime}_{0}-\ell^{\prime}}\binom{j^{\prime}}{\ell^{\prime}}\cdot\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{U}^{\dagger}(\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi})\big)_{j^{\prime}}\cdot\eta_{1}(w)^{-j^{\prime}}.

(
6.22.1
) follows from dividing the above congruence by
p^{\tfrac{1}{2}(\deg(\underline{\zeta})-\deg(\underline{\xi}))}\cdot\tilde{g}_{n}(w)
and then taking the coefficients of
(w-w_{k})^{n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-j^{\prime}_{0}-1}
.
∎

6.23.
Proof of Theorem
6.13
assuming Proposition
6.20

We continue with the inductive proof of Theorem
6.13
initiated in §
6.19
. We fix the integer
\ell
as in Theorem
6.13
and we prove (
6.13.1
) by induction on
i
. Fix
i_{0}\in\{0,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
and assume that (
6.13.1
) holds for every nonnegative integer
i<i_{0}
. Set
j_{0}=n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-i_{0}-1
. Then

•

when
n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, we simply have
j_{0}\geq m_{n}(k)-i_{0}-1\geq 0
, and

•

when
n\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
, we have

m_{n}(k)=d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}-n\geq i_{0}+1
and hence
j_{0}\geq 2n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
.

For
j_{0}<j\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
, we have
m_{n-j}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
and
m_{n-j}(k)=n-j-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}\leq i_{0}
by Remark
6.9
. Therefore we can apply Proposition
6.8
to
B_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}
’s and get

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\frac{k-2}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)
for all such
j
’s. By Proposition
6.20
, we also have
v_{p}\big(C_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\frac{k-2}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)
for all such
j
’s.

As noted at the beginning of §
6.19
, when
\ell>j_{0}
, (
6.13.1
) already follows from the weak estimate in Proposition
6.8
. So we can assume
\ell\leq j_{0}
. We apply (
6.22.1
) to
\ell^{\prime}=\ell
and
j_{0}^{\prime}=j_{0}
, and deduce that
C_{k,i_{0}}^{(\ell)}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear combination of
C_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}
’s with
j_{0}<j\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
. From the above discussion, we have
v_{p}\big(C_{k,i_{0}}^{(\ell)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\frac{k-2}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)
. By Proposition
6.20
we get
v_{p}\big(B_{k,i_{0}}^{(\ell)}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\frac{k-2}{2}(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n)
. This completes the inductive proof of Theorem
6.13
, and hence conclude the proof of the local ghost Theorem
2.8
(assuming Proposition
6.20
).

6.24.
Proof of Proposition
6.20

We now come back to prove this last missing piece for the proof of Theorem
6.13
and the local ghost Theorem
2.8
.
For every
0\leq j\leq n
, we consider the following formal expansion in
E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket
:

\frac{\eta_{j}(w)}{\eta_{1}(w)^{j}}=1+\eta_{(j),1}(w-w_{k})+\eta_{(j),2}(w-w_{k})^{2}+\cdots\in E\llbracket w-w_{k}\rrbracket.

The key result to prove Proposition
6.20
is the following estimate on the coefficients in the above expansion:

Proposition 6.25
.

(1)

Suppose that
j
is a nonnegative integer such that
j\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
and that
1\leq m_{n-j}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
(in particular
j<n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
).
For every
t\in\{1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
, set
q_{t}:=\min\{m_{n}(k)-t,m_{n-j}(k)\}
. Then we have

(6.25.1)

v_{p}(\eta_{(j),t})\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}\big).

(2)

Suppose that
j
is a nonnegative integer such that
j\leq r_{\underline{\zeta}\times\underline{\xi}}+s_{\underline{\xi}}
and that
m_{n-j}(k)=0
(this implies that
j\geq n-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}
).
Then for every
t\in\{1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
, we have

(6.25.2)

v_{p}(\eta_{(j),t})\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t)^{2}\big).

We will first prove Proposition
6.20
assuming Proposition
6.25
and then return to prove Proposition
6.25
in §
6.27
.

Lemma 6.26
.

Proposition
6.25
implies Proposition
6.20
.

Proof.

Proposition
6.20
is trivial for
j=0
and we assume
j>0
from now on.
From the definition of
C_{k,i}^{(j)}
in (
6.19.2
), we have

C_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}=B_{k,i_{0}}^{(j)}+\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}-1}B_{k,i}^{(j)}\cdot\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i}.

To prove Proposition
6.20
, it suffices to prove

v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(j)}\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big),

for every
0\leq i<i_{0}
. We fix such an
i
and set
t=i_{0}-i
. We consider separately two cases:

(1)

Assume
i<m_{n-j}(k)
. In particular
m_{n-j}(k)\geq 1
so Proposition
6.25
(1) applies.

Since
i<m_{n-j}(k)
or equivalently,
i_{0}<t+m_{n-j}(k)
, for the
q_{t}
defined in Proposition
6.25
(1), we have
q_{t}+t=\min\{m_{n}(k),m_{n-j}(k)+t\}>i_{0}
as
m_{n}(k)>i_{0}
. It follows from the convexity of
\underline{\Delta}_{k}
that
\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}
. By (
6.25.1
) we have
v_{p}\big(\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i}\big)=v_{p}\big(\eta_{(j),t}\big)\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}
. Combining this with the assumption on
v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(j)}\big)
gives

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(j)}\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i}\big)\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)+\big(\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)+\big(\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i}\big)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

(2)

Assume
i\geq m_{n-j}(k)
. We want to write (
6.25.1
) and (
6.25.2
) uniformly as

v_{p}(\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i})=v_{p}(\eta_{(j),t})\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}\big).

For this, if
m_{n-j}=0
, we just need to define
q_{t}=0
;
if
m_{n-j}(k)\geq 1
, we need to show that
m_{n-j}(k)\leq m_{n}(k)-1
: indeed,
m_{n-j}(k)+t=m_{n-j}(k)-i+i_{0}\leq i_{0}<m_{n}(k)
. In addition, in either case, we have

q_{t}+t=\min\{m_{n}(k),m_{n-j}(k)+i_{0}-i\}=m_{n-j}(k)+i_{0}-i\leq i_{0}.

On the other hand, the strong estimates (
6.8.1
) and (
6.8.3
) can also be written uniformly as

v_{p}(B_{k,i}^{(j)})\geq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big).

So we have

\displaystyle v_{p}\big(B_{k,i}^{(j)}\eta_{(j),i_{0}-i}\big)\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i)^{2}\big)-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)

\displaystyle\geq\

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0})^{2}\big)-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{E:Delta - Delta' geq half of diff square}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-i_{0}}-\tfrac{k-2}{2}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n\big).

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.27.
Proof of Proposition
6.25

The proposition is trivial for
j=0
and
j=1
. We assume
j\geq 2
from now on.
By the definition of
\eta_{j}
in (
6.17.1
) we can write

\eta_{j}(w)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}\equiv k_{\varepsilon}\bmod(p-1)\\
k^{\prime}\neq k\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{w-w_{k}}{w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}}}\Big)^{m_{n-j}(k^{\prime})-m_{n}(k^{\prime})}\text{~and hence~}

(6.27.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{j}(w)}{\eta_{1}(w)^{j}}\

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}\equiv k_{\varepsilon}\bmod(p-1)\\
k^{\prime}\neq k\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{w-w_{k}}{w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}}}\Big)^{m_{n-j}(k^{\prime})-m_{n}(k^{\prime})-j(m_{n-1}(k^{\prime})-m_{n}(k^{\prime}))}

\displaystyle=1+\eta_{(j),1}(w-w_{k})+\eta_{(j),2}(w-w_{k})^{2}+\cdots.

Set
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}):=m_{n-j}(k^{\prime})-m_{n}(k^{\prime})-j(m_{n-1}(k^{\prime})-m_{n}(k^{\prime}))
. The term
\big(1+\tfrac{w-w_{k}}{w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}}}\big)^{m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})}
appearing in the product of (
6.27.1
) is not
1
only when the function
n^{\prime}\mapsto m_{n^{\prime}}(k^{\prime})
for
n^{\prime}\in[n-j,n]
fails to be linear, or equivalently, at least one of
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
,
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
, or
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
belongs to
(n-j,n)
. We call such weights
k^{\prime}

bad weights
. By (
6.27.1
), for
t\in\{1,\dots,m_{n}(k)-1\}
,
\eta_{(j),t}
is the sum of terms of the form

(6.27.2)

\prod_{\alpha=1}^{t}\frac{1}{w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}}},

where
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}
’s are weights satisfying the following constraints:

•

if
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}_{\alpha})>0
, the multiplicity of
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}
appearing in (
6.27.2
) is less or equal to
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}_{\alpha})
;

•

if
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}_{\alpha})<0
, the term
\big(1+\tfrac{w-w_{k}}{w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}}}\big)^{m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})}
appearing in (
6.27.1
) is considered as a Taylor expansion, so there is no constraint on the multiplicity of
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}
in (
6.27.2
).

From the above discussion, we reduce the proof of Proposition
6.25
to the following:

Lemma 6.28
.

Let
\mathcal{S}=\{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}\,|\,\alpha=1,\dots,t\}
be a set of (not necessarily distinct) bad weights satisfying that, for every
\alpha\in\{1,\dots,t\}
such that
m_{n,j}(k_{\alpha}^{\prime})>0
, the multiplicity of
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{S}
is less or equal to
m_{n,j}(k_{\alpha}^{\prime})
.

(1)

Under the assumption of Proposition
6.25
(1), we have

(6.28.1)

\sum_{\alpha=1}^{t}v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}})\leq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}-\Delta_{k,\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}\big).

(2)

Under the assumption of Proposition
6.25
(2), we have

(6.28.2)

\sum_{\alpha=1}^{t}v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}})\leq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t)^{2}\big).

The proof of this lemma will be given in §
6.30
after the following reduction.

Lemma 6.29
.

Define
n^{*}:=n
if
n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
and
n^{*}=d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n
if
n\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
. Equivalently,
n^{*}
is the unique integer satisfying
n^{*}\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
and
m_{n^{*}}(k)=m_{n}(k)
.
To prove (
6.28.1
) or (
6.28.2
), we can assume that for every bad weight
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}
, we have
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}},\,d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\notin[n^{\ast},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{\ast})
.

Proof.

Suppose that there exists some
\alpha\in\{1,\dots,t\}
such that either
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
or
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
[n^{\ast},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{\ast})
. By induction, it suffices to prove that the estimate (
6.28.1
) or (
6.28.2
) for the set
\mathcal{S}
follows from the same estimate for the set
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{S}\setminus\{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}\}
.

Case 1
: Keep the setup as in Proposition
6.25
(1). Recall
q_{t}=\min\{m_{n}(k)-t,m_{n-j}(k)\}
.

When
m_{n}(k)-t\geq m_{n-j}(k)
, we have
q_{t}=q_{t-1}=m_{n-j}(k)
and
q_{t}+t\leq m_{n}(k)
. Comparing (
6.28.1
) for
\mathcal{S}
and for
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, and setting
s:=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-t+1
, we need to prove that

(6.29.1)

v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\Delta_{k,s}-\Delta_{k,s-1}-\tfrac{1}{2}(s^{2}-(s-1)^{2}).

But by Lemma
4.10
(1) and the property of
n^{*}
, we have
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{*}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n^{*}}(k)=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)=s-1
. So
[n^{\ast},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{\ast})\subseteq[\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(s-1),\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+(s-1)]
. Applying Proposition
2.22
to
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
,
\ell=\ell^{\prime}=s-1<\ell^{\prime\prime}=s
exactly gives (
6.29.1
).

When
m_{n}(k)-t>m_{n-j}(k)
, we have
q_{t}=m_{n}(k)-t
and
q_{t-1}=q_{t}+1
. In this case, we need to prove, setting
s^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t+1
,

(6.29.2)

v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\Delta_{k,s^{\prime}}-\Delta_{k,s^{\prime}-1}-\tfrac{1}{2}(s^{\prime 2}-(s^{\prime}-1)^{2}).

Similarly, we observe
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{*}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-1=s-1
. We still have
[n^{*},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{*})\subseteq[\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(s^{\prime}-1),\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+(s^{\prime}-1)]
.
Applying Proposition
2.22
to
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
,
\ell=\ell^{\prime}=s^{\prime}-1<\ell^{\prime\prime}=s^{\prime}
proves (
6.29.2
).

Case 2
: Keep the setup as in Proposition
6.25
(2). Set
s^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t+1
; we need to show

(6.29.3)

v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\Delta_{k,s^{\prime\prime}}-\Delta_{k,s^{\prime\prime}-1}-\tfrac{1}{2}(s^{\prime\prime 2}-(s^{\prime\prime}-1)^{2}).

Again,
t<m_{n}(k)
implies that
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{\ast}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t=s^{\prime\prime}-1
. This gives
[n^{*},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{*})\subset[\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(s^{\prime\prime}-1),\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+(s^{\prime\prime}-1)]
. Applying Proposition
2.22
to
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
,
\ell=\ell^{\prime}=s^{\prime\prime}-1<\ell^{\prime\prime}=s^{\prime\prime}
proves (
6.29.3
).
∎

6.30.
Proof of Lemma
6.28

By Lemma
6.29
, we assume that, for every bad weight
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}
,
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}},\,d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\notin[n^{\ast},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n^{\ast})
. We further assume that
t\geq 1
, as there is nothing to prove when
t=0
. We separate two cases.

Case 1
: Keep the setup as in Proposition
6.25
(1).
We first explain that
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\geq 2
(recall that
q_{t}=\min\{m_{n}(k)-t,m_{n-j}(k)\}
). Indeed, if
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\in\{0,1\}
, we must have
t=1
and
m_{n}(k)-1=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-1=m_{n-j}(k)
. The first equality implies that
n=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
. But we have
j\geq 2
by earlier assumption, which implies that
m_{n-j}(k)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-2
, contradicting with the second equality above. So we always have
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\geq 2
.

Set
\gamma\coloneqq\big\lfloor\frac{\ln((p+1)(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}))}{\ln p}+1\big\rfloor
. We next show that if some bad weight
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}
satisfies
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\in(n-j,n)
, then
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\gamma
. In fact, if
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\leq n
, we have
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\in(n-j,n)
and hence
|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\alpha\bullet}|=|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|<j
. By Remark
6.14
we have
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-j}(k)\geq\frac{j}{2}
. Therefore
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})=1+v_{p}(k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\alpha\bullet})\leq 1+\lfloor\frac{\ln j}{\ln p}\rfloor\leq\gamma
. If
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}>n
, we have

\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n-j}(k)=(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k))+j
. On the other hand,
|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\alpha\bullet}|=|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n+|n-\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-m_{n}(k)+j
. So we also have
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\gamma
.

(a)

Assume
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\gamma
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\dots,t\}
. Since
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}\geq 2
, we can apply Proposition
2.22
to
\ell=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)<\ell^{\prime}=\ell^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}
, to deduce that

\sum_{\alpha=1}^{t}v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}})\leq t\cdot\gamma\leq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}\big),

which gives (
6.28.1
) in this case.

(b)

Assume
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}})\geq\gamma+1
for some
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
. We can assume that the multiplicity of
k^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{S}
is
M>0
and
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}=k^{\prime}
for
\alpha\in\{t-M+1,\dots,t\}
. By the assumption at the beginning of the proof and the discussion just before (a), we know that
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\notin(n-j,n)
, and either
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
or
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
(n-j,n^{*})
. By Remark
2.23
,
k^{\prime}
must be the unique element in
\mathcal{S}
with the properties that
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}})\geq\gamma+1
and either
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
or
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
\big(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}),\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})\big)
.

When
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\in(n-j,n^{\ast})
, we have
n-j<d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}<n\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
(because
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\not\in(n-j,n)
) and hence
m_{n-j}(k^{\prime})=0
,
m_{n}(k^{\prime})=n-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
, and
m_{n-1}(k^{\prime})=m_{n}(k^{\prime})-1
. It follows that
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})=d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}-(n-j)>0
and

(6.30.1)

\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n+j-m_{n,j}(k)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}).

When
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\in(n-j,n^{\ast})
, we have
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\leq n-j<d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}<n
(again because
\frac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\not\in(n-j,n)
) and hence
m_{n-j}(k^{\prime})=d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}-(n-j)>0
and
m_{n-1}(k^{\prime})=m_{n}(k^{\prime})=0
. It follows that
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})=d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}-(n-j)
and

(6.30.2)

\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}})=\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-n+j-m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})\leq\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-m_{n,j}(k^{\prime}).

In summary, we always have
m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})>0
and we deduce a key inequality
M\leq m_{n,j}(k^{\prime})
by the condition of Lemma
6.28
. By (
6.30.1
) and (
6.30.2
), either
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
or
d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
[\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-M),\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}+(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-M)]
. Now we can apply Proposition
2.22
to
k^{\prime}
,
\ell=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)
,
\ell^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}-M
, and
\ell^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}
, to deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{t}v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})

\displaystyle\leq(t-M)\cdot\gamma+M\cdot v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}})\leq\Delta_{k,\ell^{\prime\prime}}-\Delta_{k,\ell}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\ell^{\prime\prime 2}-\ell^{2})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-q_{t})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-(q_{t}+t))^{2}\big),

which gives (
6.28.1
) in this case.

Case 2
: Keep the setup as in Proposition
6.25
(2). Every bad weight
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
satisfies one of the following conditions: (a)
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\in(n-j,n)\subset(n-j,d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}})
; (b) either
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
or
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
(n-j,n^{\ast})\subset(n-j,\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})
. Note that if
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}\in[d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}},d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}})
,
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
satisfies condition
(1)
in Lemma
2.24
; if one of the integers
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
and
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
belongs to
(n-j,d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}})
or if
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}-d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
belongs to
(n-j,\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})
,
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
satisfies condition
(2)
in Lemma
2.24
; if
d_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\in[d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}},\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}})
,
k_{\alpha}^{\prime}
satisfies condition
(3)
in Lemma
2.24
. By Lemma
2.24
, if we set
\gamma\coloneqq\big\lfloor\frac{\ln((p+1)(\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}))}{\ln p}+1\big\rfloor
, we have
v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\leq\gamma
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\dots,t\}
. Note that if
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}=1
, we have
m_{n}(k)\leq 1
and Proposition
6.25
(2) is trivial. So we can assume
\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}\geq 2
and then apply Proposition
2.22
to
\ell=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t<\ell^{\prime}=\ell^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}
and we get

\sum_{\alpha=1}^{t}v_{p}(w_{k}-w_{k_{\alpha}^{\prime}})\leq t\cdot\gamma\leq\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}}-\Delta_{k,\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big((\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}})^{2}-(\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}-t)^{2}\big),

which gives (
6.28.2
).

Now we complete the proof of Lemma
6.28
.
\Box

With Lemma
6.28
proved, we complete the proof of Proposition
6.25
and Theorem
2.8
.

7.
Trianguline deformation space and crystalline slopes

In this section, we recall the trianguline deformation space defined by Breuil–Hellman–Schraen
[
BHS17
]
in §
7.3
and Paškūnas module in §
7.14
. We then compare the trianguline deformation space with the eigenvariety attached to Paškūnas’ universal deformation of representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

[
Pa13
]
in §
7.17
. This together with the known
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
allows us to transport the local ghost theorem to results regarding slopes on trianguline deformation spaces (see Theorem
7.6
below).

The argument in this section is relatively well known to experts, but some of the awkward arguments are inserted to treat central characters for completeness.

Notation 7.1
.

As in previous sections, let
p
be an odd prime, and let
E,\mathcal{O},\mathbb{F}
be coefficient rings as in §
1.27
.
For a formal
\mathcal{O}
-scheme
\Spf(R)
, let
\Spf(R)^{\mathrm{rig}}
denote the associated rigid analytic space over
E
.
We will later frequently write
E^{\prime}
to mean a finite extension of
E
, typically in the situation of referring to a point of
\Spf(R)^{\mathrm{rig}}
over
E^{\prime}
; we will freely do so without defining
E^{\prime}
, and in such case, we use
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
,
\varpi^{\prime}
, and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
denote the corresponding ring of integers, a uniformizer, and the residue field, respectively.

For a crystabelline representation
V
of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(with coefficients in
E^{\prime}
), write
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(V)
for the limit of the crystalline functor over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{n}})
with
n
sufficiently large.

We normalize the local class field theory so that the Artin map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{ab}}
sends
p
to the
geometric
Frobenius. In what follows, we will practically identify characters of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
(with values in
\mathcal{O}^{\times}
or
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
) and characters of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

We recall the following notations for local Galois representations:

•

For
R
a
p
-adically complete ring and
\alpha\in R^{\times}
, write
\mathrm{unr}(\alpha):\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to R^{\times}
for the unramified representation sending the geometric Frobenius element to
\alpha
.

•

Let
\omega_{1}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
denote the
first fundamental character
.

•

Let
\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\subset\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{ab}}\to\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
denote the cyclotomic character; its reduction modulo
p
is precisely
\omega_{1}
.

Recall
\Delta:=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
, the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}\llbracket(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\rrbracket\cong\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket
, and the universal character
\chi_{\mathrm{univ}}^{(\varepsilon)}:\Delta\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon),\times}
associated to a character
\varepsilon
of
\Delta^{2}
from §
2.4
(1).
For each
\varepsilon
, call
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}:=(\Spf\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)})^{\mathrm{rig}}
the
weight space labeled by
\varepsilon
.
Put
\mathcal{W}:=\bigcup_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
; it parameterizes continuous characters of
\Delta\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Write
\chi_{\mathrm{univ}}:\Delta\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
for the universal character.
Put
\mathcal{W}_{0}:=(\Spf\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket)^{\mathrm{rig}}
, parameterizing continuous characters of
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}:=(\Spf\mathcal{O}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket)^{\mathrm{rig}}
be the rigid analytic space parameterizing continuous characters of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}
. There is a natural isomorphism

(7.1.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{W}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi,\eta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\ (\alpha,\delta)\mapsto\alpha\cdot\chi(\bar{\delta},\alpha)\cdot\eta(\alpha\delta\omega(\bar{\alpha}\bar{\delta})^{-1})\ \textrm{ for }\alpha,\delta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Here, we used
\chi(\bar{\delta},\alpha)
as opposed to
\chi(\bar{\alpha},\delta)
because our later convention uses the lower triangular matrix local analytic Jacquet functor. The additional factor
\alpha
at the beginning indicates a twist by cyclotomic character in our convention.

Under this isomorphism, we may view
\mathcal{W}
as a subspace of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
where the universal character is trivial on
\{1\}\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
; and at the same time, we have a projection map
\mathrm{pr}_{W}:\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\to\mathcal{W}
, along
\mathcal{W}_{0}
.

Later, we often consider a rigid analytic space
\mathcal{X}
and the morphism
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{X}}\times\mathrm{pr}_{W}:\mathcal{X}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{W}
; we write
\mathrm{pr}_{W}
for it when no confusion arises.

Notation 7.2
.

For the rest of this paper, we use
\bar{r}_{p}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
to denote a reducible and generic residual representation

\bar{r}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{1})\omega_{1}^{a+b+1}&*\\
0&\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{2})\omega_{1}^{b}\end{pmatrix}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})

with
a\in\{1,\dots,p-4\}
,
b\in\{0,\dots,p-2\}
, and
\bar{\alpha}_{1},\bar{\alpha}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
. We say
\bar{r}_{p}
is
split
if
*=0
and
nonsplit
if
*\neq 0
. The genericity condition on
a
ensures that there is a unique such nontrivial extension when
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit.

Write the associated Serre weight (a
right

\mathbb{F}[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})]
-module)
\boldsymbol{\sigma}=\sigma_{a,b}=\Sym^{a}\mathbb{F}^{\oplus 2}\otimes\mathrm{det}^{b}
. (It is the unique Serre weight for
\bar{r}_{p}
when the extension is nonsplit.)

We occasionally use a companion representation

\bar{r}^{\prime}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{1})\omega_{1}^{a+b+1}&0\\
*\neq 0&\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{2})\omega_{1}^{b}\end{pmatrix}

This will change the parameters
(a,b)
to
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=(p-3-a,a+b+1)
. The corresponding Serre weight is
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{p-3-a,a+b+1}
.

7.3.
Trianguline deformation spaces

Let
\mathcal{T}
denote the rigid analytic space parameterizing continuous characters of
(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times})^{2}
, or more precisely,

(7.3.1)

\mathcal{T}=\big(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}\times(\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rrbracket)^{\mathrm{rig}}\big)^{2}\cong(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}})^{2}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}},

where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}=\bigcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}\Spm\big(\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle\frac{u}{p^{n}},\frac{p^{n}}{u}\rangle\big)
is the rigid analytic
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
The point on
\mathcal{T}
associated to a character
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2}):(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
is
(\delta_{1}(p),\delta_{2}(p),\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}},\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})
.
There is a natural
weight map

\mathrm{wt}:\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
.
Define
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{reg}}
to be the Zariski open subspace of
\mathcal{T}
, where neither
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}
nor
\delta_{2}/\delta_{1}
is
a character of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
in the following list:

x\mapsto x^{n}\textrm{ and }x\mapsto x^{n}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}(x)\textrm{ with }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Let
\bar{r}_{p}
be as in Notation
7.2
. Let
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
denote the framed deformation ring of
\bar{r}_{p}
parameterizing deformations of
\bar{r}_{p}
into matrix representations of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
with coefficients in complete noetherian local
\mathcal{O}
-algebras. Then the Krull dimension of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
is
9
. Let
V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\square}
denote the universal (matrix) representation over
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
denote the rigid analytic space over
E
associated to the formal scheme
\Spf R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
; it has dimension
8
. Write
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\square}
for the associated universal representation over
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
. For a point
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
over
E^{\prime}
, write
\mathcal{V}_{x}
for universal Galois representation of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
E^{\prime}
at
x
.

Following
[
BHS17
, Definition 2.4]
, we define the trianguline deformation space as follows.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
U_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
denote the set of closed points
(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{reg}}
(with some residue field
E^{\prime}
) such that the associated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
sits in an exact sequence

(7.4.1)

0\to\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{1})\to\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{V}_{x})\to\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{2})\to 0,

where
\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}
is the Robba ring for
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with coefficients in
E^{\prime}
; see
[
KPX14
, § 6]
and
[
Li15
]
for the notation
\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(-)
and related discussions on triangulations of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules.

The
trianguline deformation space of
\bar{r}_{p}
, denoted by
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, is the Zariski closure of
U_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
inside the product
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

(1)

The space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
is a subspace of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
consisting of points
(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
for which
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
corresponds to
\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
under local class field theory.
Moreover, set

\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri},\circ}:=\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}\cap\big(\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}})^{2}\times\mathcal{W}\big)
, then (
7.1.1
) induces an isomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri},\circ}\times\mathcal{W}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\mathcal{V}_{x},\delta_{1},\delta_{2}),\eta\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{x}\otimes\eta,\delta_{1}\otimes\eta,\delta_{2}\otimes\eta),}

which is compatible with projections to the factor
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}})^{2}
.

(2)

The set
U_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
is the set of closed points of a Zariski open and dense subspace
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. The space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
is equidimensional of dimension
7
.

Proof.

(1) obviously holds for points in
U_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
and hence for
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. (2) is proved in
[
BHS17
, Théorèm 2.6]
.
∎

The main theorem of this section is the following.

Theorem 7.6
.

Assume that
p\geq 11
. Let
\bar{r}_{p}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
be a residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
2\leq a\leq p-5
, and let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
be the Serre weight therein. Let
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
be the trianguline deformation space defined above.
For every
E^{\prime}
-point
\underline{x}=(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, we have

(a)

the character
\varepsilon:=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}
is relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, and

(b)

the image of
\underline{x}
in
\mathcal{W}
under
\mathrm{pr}_{W}
is
w_{\star}:=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))-1
.

Then the following statement holds.

(1)

If
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=-v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))>0
, then
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))
is equal to a slope appearing in the Newton polygon
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
.

(2)

If
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=0
, then either
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
, or
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
and
\bar{r}_{p}|_{\mathrm{I}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is split.

(3)

If
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=\frac{k-2}{2}
and
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}=\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{k-1}\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
for some integer
k\geq 2
, then
\delta_{1}(p)=p^{k-2}\delta_{2}(p)
.

Conversely, fix characters
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
and
\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
such that
\varepsilon
defined above is relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
. Then every nonzero slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
for
w_{\star}:=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))-1
, appears as
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))
at some closed point
\underline{x}=(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
(for some continuous characters
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
extending the given
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
and
\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
).

The proof of this theorem will occupy the rest of this section, and is concluded in §
7.22
. We quickly remark that case (1) corresponds to the case when
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is reducible, and case (3) mostly concerns the case when
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is semistable and noncrystalline (after a twist).

Temporarily admitting this theorem, we first deduce a couple of corollaries that partially answer a conjecture of Breuil–Buzzard–Emerton on crystalline slopes of Kisin’s crystabelline deformation spaces and a conjecture of Gouvêa on slopes of crystalline deformation spaces.

7.7.
Kisin’s crystabelline deformation space

Let
\bar{r}_{p}
,
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
, and
V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\square}
be as above.
Let
\underline{\psi}=\psi_{1}\times\psi_{2}:(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\to E^{\times}
be a finite character (enlarging
E
if needed to contain the image of
\underline{\psi}
), and let
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with
k_{1}<k_{2}
be a pair of Hodge–Tate weights. (In our convention,
\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}
has Hodge–Tate weight
-1
.) In
[
Kis08
]
, Kisin proved that there is a unique
\mathcal{O}
-flat quotient
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
of
R^{\square}_{\bar{r}_{p}}
, called
the Kisin’s crystabelline deformation ring
, such that every homomorphism
x^{*}:R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\to E^{\prime}
factors through
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
if and only if
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is potentially crystalline with Hodge–Tate weights
(k_{1},k_{2})
and the action of
\mathrm{I}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
on
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
is isomorphic to
\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}
. (Here
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(-)
is defined in Notation
7.1
.) When
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
is nonempty, each of its irreducible component has
Krull dimension
6
. Moreover, the associated rigid analytic space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}:=\big(\Spf R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}
is smooth of dimension
5
over
E
.

Corollary 7.8
.

Assume that
p\geq 11
.
Let
\bar{r}_{p}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
be a residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
2\leq a\leq p-5
, and let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
be the Serre weight therein. Let
\underline{\psi}
and
\underline{k}
be as above, and let
x
be an
E^{\prime}
-point of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
. Let
\alpha_{x}
be an eigenvalue of the
\phi
-action on the subspace of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
where
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts through
\psi_{1}
. Write
w_{\star}:=(\psi_{2}\psi_{1}^{-1})(\exp(p))\cdot\exp(p(k_{2}-k_{1}-1))-1
(for the image of
x
in
\mathcal{W}
under
\mathrm{pr}_{W}
).
Then the character
\varepsilon:=\psi_{2}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-k_{2}}\times\psi_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-k_{1}-1}
is relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, and

(1)

if
k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\notin\{0,k_{2}-k_{1}\}
, then it is equal to a slope appearing in
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
;

(2)

if
v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\in\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
, then
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is reducible; and

(3)

in the special case
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
, we have
v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\neq\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}{2}
.

Conversely, every slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
belonging to
(0,k_{2}-k_{1})
(but not equal to
\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}{2}
when
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
) appears as the
k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{x})
at some point
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
.

Proof.

(2) If
v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\in\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
, the standard
p
-adic Hodge theory implies that
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is reducible.

(1) Assume that
v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\notin\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
.
This essentially follows from Theorem
7.6
because all crystabelline representations are trianguline. More precisely, let
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}(E^{\prime})
be a closed point. By possibly replacing
E^{\prime}
by a quadratic extension,
the action of crystalline Frobenius
\phi
and
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
have two (generalized) eigencharacters:
(\alpha_{1},\psi_{1})
and
(\alpha_{2},\psi_{2})
, with
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
in the data defining the deformation space and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in E^{\prime\times}
. We assume that
(\alpha_{1},\psi_{1})
is a genuine eigencharacter. Define characters
\delta_{i}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to E^{\prime\times}
with
i=1,2
by

\delta_{i}(p)=p^{-k_{i}}{\alpha_{3-i}},\quad\delta_{i}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}=x^{-k_{i}}\psi_{3-i}.

See §
1.28
for our convention which explains why we use
\alpha_{3-i}
and
\psi_{3-i}
here.
Standard facts of Berger’s functor give rise to a triangulation

(7.8.1)

0\to\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{1})\to\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})\to\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{2})\to 0.

(Indeed, if not, it must be that the eigenspace for
(\alpha_{2},\psi_{2})
agrees with
\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{2}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
; then the admissibility condition for
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
forces
v_{p}(\alpha_{1})=k_{2}
, contradicting our assumption.)

Now, (
7.8.1
) upgrades
x
to a point
(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, for which
v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))=v_{p}(\alpha_{1})-k_{2}
. (1) follows from Theorem
7.6
, with

(7.8.2)

w_{\star}:=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))-1=(\psi_{2}\psi_{1}^{-1})(\exp(p))\exp(p(k_{2}-k_{1}-1))-1.

(3) Assume that
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
. Suppose that the subspace
\mathcal{Y}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
where
v_{p}(\alpha_{x})=\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}{2}
is nonempty. Then this is a smooth rigid analytic subdomain, in particular,
\dim\mathcal{Y}=5
.
This dimension can be also seen as follows: let
x
be a closed point of
\mathcal{Y}
. The dimension of the tangent space of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
at
x
is equal to
1+3+\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathrm{Ad}(\mathcal{V}_{x}))
, where
1
comes from infinitesimal central twist of
\mathcal{V}_{x}
by an unramified character,
3
comes from the framing variables, and the one-dimensional
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathrm{Ad}(\mathcal{V}_{x}))
corresponds to varying the ratio of two Frobenius eigenvalues.

However, for such
x\in\mathcal{Y}
,
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}=\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{k_{2}-k_{1}}\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
. Theorem
7.6
(3) implies that
\delta_{1}(p)=p^{k_{2}-k_{1}-1}\delta_{2}(p)
. This means that
\mathcal{Y}
is confined in the subspace where the ratio of two Frobenius eigenvalues on
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
is precisely
p
. This contradicts with the earlier dimension computation of the tangent space at
x
. (3) is proved.

Conversely, given a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
belonging to
(0,k_{2}-k_{1})
(and not being equal to
\frac{k_{2}-k_{1}}{2}-1
when
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
), Theorem
7.6
defines a triangulation (
7.8.1
) with
\mathcal{V}_{x}
having the reduction
\bar{r}_{p}
. The slope condition implies that (
7.8.1
) belongs to the type
\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{cris}}_{+}
in
[
Colm08
]
. So
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is crystabelline.
∎

Remark 7.9
.

(1)

We omitted a full discussion when
\alpha_{x}\in\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
, which is a standard exercise in
p
-adic Hodge theory.

(2)

(Possibly up to replacing
E
by a degree
2
extension when
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
), it is possible to embed
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\underline{k},\underline{\psi}}
into
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
as a rigid analytic subspace, but this construction is a little messy to present, in the ordinary, critical, or Frobenius non-semisimple cases. We content ourselves with a pointwise description and leave the “global” argument to interested readers.

The following answers positively a conjecture by Breuil–Buzzard–Emerton, and a conjecture of Gouvêa, when the residual Galois representation is reducible and generic. We refer to §
1.9
and §
1.12
for the discussion on their history, and Remarks
1.11
and
1.14
for comments on previous related works.

Corollary 7.10
.

Assume that
p\geq 11
.
Let
\bar{r}_{p}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
be a residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
2\leq a\leq p-5
. Let
\underline{\psi}
,
\underline{k}
,
x
,
\alpha_{x}
be as in Corollary
7.8
.

(1)

If

m
denotes the minimal
positive
integer such that
\psi_{1}\psi_{2}^{-1}
is trivial on
(1+p^{m}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, then

v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\in\begin{cases}\big(\frac{a}{2}+\mathbb{Z}\big)\cup\mathbb{Z}&\textrm{ when }m=1,\\
\frac{1}{(p-1)p^{m-1}}\mathbb{Z}&\textrm{ when }m\geq 2.\end{cases}

(2)

If
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
, then

v_{p}(\alpha_{x})-k_{1}\textrm{ or }k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{x})\textrm{ belongs to }\bigg[0,\,\Big\lfloor\frac{k_{2}-k_{1}-1-\min\{a+1,p-2-a\}}{p+1}\Big\rfloor\bigg].

Proof.

(1)
When
m=1
, this follows from Corollary
7.8

and Proposition
2.20
(6).
When
m\geq 2
, we have
v_{p}(w_{\star})=\frac{1}{(p-1)p^{m-1}}
, and the slopes of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
are precisely
v_{p}(w_{\star})\cdot\big(\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{n-1}^{(\varepsilon)}\big)
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
with multiplicity one, by the second last line of Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4). In this case, (1) follows from this and Corollary
7.8
.

(2) If
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
, then
k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{1})
is a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k_{2}-k_{1}+1},-)\big)
which is not
\frac{k_{2}-k_{1}-1}{2}
. By Proposition
2.17
(3)(4), either
k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{x})
belongs to
\big[0,\,\big\lfloor\frac{k_{2}-k_{1}-1-\min\{a+1,p-2-a\}}{p+1}\big\rfloor\big]
, or
(k_{2}-k_{1})-(k_{2}-v_{p}(\alpha_{x}))=v_{p}(\alpha_{x})-k_{1}
belongs to this set.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to proving Theorem
7.6
, which is completed in §
7.22
.

7.11.
Reducing Theorem
7.6
to the nonsplit case

We first show that Theorem
7.6
for
\bar{r}_{p}
nonsplit implies the theorem for
\bar{r}_{p}
split. This is essentially because, at least pointwise for an irreducible trianguline representation, there are lattices for which the reductions are extensions of the two characters in either order.

To make this precise, we first note that the character
\varepsilon:=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}
is always relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
by considering the
\mathrm{det}\mathcal{V}_{x}
. Next, by twisting all representations by
\omega\circ\omega_{1}^{-b}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, we may reduce to the case when
b=0
.

Now suppose that Theorem
7.6
holds for nonsplit residual local Galois representations. Let
\bar{r}_{p}
be a split residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
*=0
and
b=0
. Then there is a unique nonsplit residual local Galois representation
\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}}
which is an extension of
\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{2})
by
\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{1})\omega^{a+1}
. Write
\boldsymbol{\sigma}=\sigma_{a,0}
as in Notation
7.2
.

Let
\underline{x}=(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
be an
E^{\prime}
-point of
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. (It is enough to verify Theorem
7.6
for this Zariski open subspace
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, because for every point
\underline{x}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, there is an affinoid subdomain containing
x
on which
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))
is constant and such subdomain intersects nontrivially with
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
by Proposition
7.5
(2).)
We separate two cases.

(1) If
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is irreducible, then it is well known that, after possibly enlarging
E^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{V}_{x}
admits an
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
-lattice
\mathcal{V}^{\circ}_{x}
such that
\mathcal{V}_{x}^{\circ}/\varpi^{\prime}\mathcal{V}_{x}^{\circ}\simeq\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}}
(because there is a unique extension of the two characters in
\bar{r}_{p}
). It follows that
\underline{x}^{\prime}:=(\mathcal{V}^{\circ}_{x},\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
defines a point on
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. Theorem
7.6
for
\underline{x}^{\prime}
implies Theorem
7.6
for
\underline{x}
.

(2)
If
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is reducible, i.e. there exists an exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{V}_{x}^{+}\to\mathcal{V}_{x}\to\mathcal{V}_{x}^{-}\to 0
of representations of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. There are two possibilities:

(2a)

If
\delta_{1}(p)\in\mathcal{O}^{\prime\times}
, then (
7.4.1
) produces an exact sequence of Galois representations. In particular,
\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{1})
is isomorphic to either
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathcal{V}_{x}^{+})
or
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathcal{V}_{x}^{-})
. This will imply that
\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}=1\times\omega^{a}
or
\omega^{a+1}\times\omega^{-1}
, directly verifying Theorem
7.6
(2).

(2b)

If
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))>0
, this falls in the case of
\mathscr{S}_{+}^{\mathrm{ord}}
per classification of trianguline representations in
[
Colm08
, § 1.2]
. In particular,
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=w(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, where

w(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}):=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
\gamma\to 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\log(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1})}{\log(\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}(\gamma))}

is the (negative of) generalized Hodge–Tate weight. (In
[
Colm08
]
, Colmez calls
w(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1})
the Hodge–Tate weight because in his convention the cyclotomic character has Hodge–Tate weight
1
.) Put
k:=w(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1})+1
. In this case, there is another triangulation

0\to t^{k-1}\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{2})\to\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathcal{V}_{x})\to t^{1-k}\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{1})\to 0,

which produces precisely the exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{V}_{x}^{+}\to\mathcal{V}_{x}\to\mathcal{V}_{x}^{-}\to 0
. This in particularly shows that
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=k-1
and that

\varepsilon=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}=\omega^{a-k+2}\times\omega^{k-2}.

In order to verify Theorem
7.6
(1), we will show that,
k-1
is a slope in
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, (by directly exhibiting such a slope).
There are two subcases we need to consider.

(2bi)

If
\delta_{1}|_{(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}=\delta_{2}|_{(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}
, then
w_{\star}=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))=w_{k}
. We invoke the compatibility of Atkin–Lehner involution and
p
-stabilization with ghost series in Proposition
2.17
(2)(3): the
d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\omega^{a-k+2}\times 1)
-th slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k},-)\big)
is precisely
k-1
minus the first slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})}(w_{k},-)\big)
with
s_{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}=k-2-a-(k-2-a)=0
. By Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4), the latter ghost slope is
0
, and thus the former ghost slope is
k-1
, i.e.
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))
is a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k},-)\big)
.

(2bii)

If the minimal positive integer
m
such that
\delta_{1}|_{(1+p^{m}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}=\delta_{2}|_{(1+p^{m}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}
satisfies
m\geq 2
, then we are in the “halo region”; in particular,
v_{p}(w_{\star})=\frac{1}{p^{m-2}(p-1)}
.
In this case, Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4) implies that the
n
th slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is just
\frac{1}{p^{m-2}(p-1)}\big(\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{n-1}^{(\varepsilon)}\big)
.
We compute this explicitly using the formulas in Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4) with
s_{\varepsilon}=\{k-a-2\}
,

•

If
a+s_{\varepsilon}<p-1
, note that
p^{m-1}(k-1)-1\equiv k-2\equiv a+s_{\varepsilon}\bmod(p-1)
. So for
N=\frac{p^{m-1}(k-1)-1-\{k-2\}}{p-1}+1
, we have
\mathbf{e}_{2N}^{(\varepsilon)}=e_{2}^{*}z^{p^{m-1}(k-1)-1}
. Moreover, in terms of (
2.13.1
) with
n=2N-1
, we have the congruence

2N-1-2\{k-a-2\}\equiv 2(2+\{k-2\})-1-2\{k-a-2\}\equiv 2a+3\pmod{2p}.

So we use the “otherwise case” to deduce that

\deg g_{2N}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{2N-1}^{(\varepsilon)}=\deg\mathbf{e}_{2N}^{(\varepsilon)}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{2N}^{(\varepsilon)}}{p}\Big\rfloor=p^{m-2}(p-1)(k-1).

So the
2N
th slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is
k-1
.

•

If
a+s_{\varepsilon}\geq p-1
, the argument is similar. Again, put
N=\frac{p^{m-1}(k-1)-1-\{k-2\}}{p-1}+1
; in this case, we have
\mathbf{e}_{2N-1}^{(\varepsilon)}=e_{2}^{*}z^{p^{m-1}(k-1)-1}
. In terms of (
2.13.2
) with
n=2N-2
, we note the similar congruence

\displaystyle 2N-2-2\{k-2-a\}

\displaystyle\equiv 2(1+\{k-2\})-2\{k-2-a\}

\displaystyle\equiv 2a+2-2(p-1)\equiv 2a+4\pmod{2p}.

So we use the “otherwise case” again to deduce that

\deg g_{2N-1}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{2N-2}^{(\varepsilon)}=\deg\mathbf{e}_{2N-1}^{(\varepsilon)}-\Big\lfloor\frac{\deg\mathbf{e}_{2N-1}^{(\varepsilon)}}{p}\Big\rfloor=p^{m-2}(p-1)(k-1).

This means that the
(2N-1)
th slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is
k-1
.

Up to now, we have proved Theorem
7.6
(1)–(3) for
\bar{r}_{p}
. Conversely, if
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
and
\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
are given as in Theorem
7.6
. Put
w_{\star}:=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))-1
.
Let
\lambda
be a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
.

(1)’

If
\lambda>0
, then Theorem
7.6
for the nonsplit representation
\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}}
produces an
E^{\prime}
-point
\underline{x}^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
with
v_{p}(\delta_{1}(p))=\lambda
. Reversing the argument in (1) gives the needed point of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}^{\mathrm{ns}}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
.

(2)’

If
\lambda=0
, we must have
\varepsilon=1\times\omega^{a}
. We construct a point on
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
directly. Lift
\bar{\alpha}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
for each
i=1,2
to
\delta_{i}(p)\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
. Then
\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{1})\oplus\mathcal{R}_{E^{\prime}}(\delta_{2})
is the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of
\delta_{1}\oplus\delta_{2}
, which reduces to
\bar{r}_{p}
automatically, with the correct slope and characters.

This completes the reduction of Theorem
7.6
to the reducible nonsplit and generic case.

Remark 7.12
.

(1)

Case (2bii) can be also deduced from an analogous compatibility of Atkin–Lehner involution for ghost series with wild characters. We leave that for interested readers.

(2)

It is a very interesting question to ask whether the above correspondence of points between
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ns}},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
can be made “globally” at the level of rigid analytic spaces or even at the level of formal schemes.

Assumption 7.13
.

In view of §
7.11
, we assume that
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit for the rest of this section, i.e.
\bar{r}_{p}
is a nontrivial extension of
\bar{\chi}_{2}:=\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{2})\omega_{1}^{b}
by
\bar{\chi}_{2}:=\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{1})\omega_{1}^{a+b+1}
.

7.14.
Paškūnas modules

To relate the study of local ghost series with the trianguline deformation space, we make use of the Paškūnas modules in
[
Pa13
]
for deformations of
p
-adic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. As
[
Pa13
]
mainly considers the case with a fixed central character, some of our constructions later may be slightly awkward. Similar arguments to remove central character constraints can be found in
[
BD20
, Appendix A]
and
[
CEGGPS18
]
. Let
\zeta:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
be a character that induces a character of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
by local class field theory.

•

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}^{\mathrm{pro}}
be the category of profinite
\mathcal{O}
-modules
V
with continuous
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-actions.

•

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be the category of profinite
\mathcal{O}
-modules
M
with continuous
right

\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-actions for which

–

the right
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
M
extends to a right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module structure on
M
, and

–

for every vector
v
in the Pontryagin dual
M^{\vee}:=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}(M,E/\mathcal{O})
equipped with the induced left
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action, the left
\mathcal{O}[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})]
-submodule generated by
v
is of finite length.

•

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{\zeta}
be the subcategory of
\mathfrak{C}
consisting of objects on which
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
acts by
\zeta
.

We chose to work with right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rrbracket
-actions on objects of
\mathfrak{C}
to match our definition of
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective augmented modules in Definition
2.2
. This can be easily translated from references
[
Pa13
,
Pa15
,
HP19
,
BD20
]
by considering the inverse action.

There is a natural covariant
modified Colmez functor

\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}:\mathfrak{C}_{\zeta}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}^{\mathrm{pro}},

which is compatible with taking projective limits and whose evaluation on finite length objects
M
is given by
\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(M):=\mathbf{V}(M^{\vee})^{\vee}(\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}\zeta)
, where
(-)^{\vee}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}(-,E/\mathcal{O})
is the Pontryagin duality and
\mathbf{V}(-)
is the functor defined in
[
Colm10b
]
. In particular, for two characters
\bar{\eta}_{1},\bar{\eta}_{2}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{F}^{\times}
such that
\bar{\eta}_{1}\bar{\eta}_{2}\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}=\zeta\bmod\varpi
,

\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}\Big(\Ind_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}^{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\big(\bar{\eta}_{1}\otimes\bar{\eta}_{2}\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}\big)^{\vee}\Big)\cong\bar{\eta}_{1}.

We note that for a different character
\zeta^{\prime}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
,

(7.14.1)

\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta\zeta^{\prime}}(M\otimes\zeta^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{det})\cong\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(M)\otimes\zeta^{\prime}.

We focus on the case of Assumption
7.13
. Take the earlier
\zeta
to satisfy
\zeta\equiv\omega^{a+2b}\bmod\varpi
.

Let
\pi(\bar{r}_{p})
denote the smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
over
\mathbb{F}
associated to
\bar{r}_{p}
by the mod
p
Langlands correspondence. Explicitly,

\pi(\bar{r}_{p})
is the nontrivial extension
\bar{\pi}_{1}-\bar{\pi}_{2}
with

\bar{\pi}_{1}=\Ind_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}^{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\big(\bar{\chi}_{2}\otimes\bar{\chi}_{1}\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}\big)\textrm{\quad and\quad}\bar{\pi}_{2}=\Ind_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}^{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\big(\bar{\chi}_{1}\otimes\bar{\chi}_{2}\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}\big).

In particular, we have

\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(\pi(\bar{r}_{p})^{\vee})\cong\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(\bar{\pi}_{2}^{\vee}-\bar{\pi}_{1}^{\vee})\cong(\bar{\chi}_{1}-\bar{\chi}_{2})\cong\bar{r}_{p}.

This is independent of the choice of
\zeta
and agrees with
[
Pa13
, § 8]
; yet
[
Pa15
, § 6.1]
seems to have a minor error by swapping the
\bar{\pi}_{1}
with
\bar{\pi}_{2}
, which is later corrected in
[
HP19
]
.

Let
\boldsymbol{1}_{\mathrm{tw}}
denote
\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,v\rrbracket
equipped with a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
-action where
p
acts by multiplication by
1+u
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts by multiplication by
(1+v)^{\log(a/\omega(\bar{a}))/p}
; such action extends to an action of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
via local class field theory.

As
\End_{\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(\bar{r}_{p})\cong\mathbb{F}
, the deformation problem of
\bar{r}_{p}
is representable by a complete noetherian local
\mathcal{O}
-algebra
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}
. Let
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}
denote the quotient parameterizing the deformations of
\bar{r}_{p}
with fixed determinant
\zeta
; let
\mathfrak{m}_{R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}}
denote its maximal ideal. Let
V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\zeta}
denote the universal deformation of
\bar{r}_{p}
over
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}
. It is well known that there is a (noncanonical) isomorphism

R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\simeq R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,v,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket

(with
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
framing variables),
so that the framed and unframed universal deformations of
\bar{r}_{p}
satisfy:

V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\zeta}\widehat{\boxtimes}_{\mathcal{O}}{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{tw}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket\simeq V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\square}.

Following
[
Pa13
, § 8]
, we have the following.

Theorem 7.15
.

Keep the notation as above. Let
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\twoheadrightarrow\bar{\pi}_{1}^{\vee}
be a projective envelope of
\pi_{1}^{\vee}
in
\mathfrak{C}_{\zeta}
and put
R_{\pi_{1}}^{\zeta}:=\End_{\mathfrak{C}_{\zeta}}(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta})
.

(1)

The
\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta})
can be viewed as a
2
-dimensional representation of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
R_{\pi_{1}}^{\zeta}
lifting
\bar{r}_{p}
; this induces an isomorphism
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }R_{\pi_{1}}^{\zeta}
, and
\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\zeta}(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta})\cong V_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\zeta}
.

(2)

Define the following object in
\mathfrak{C}
:

(7.15.1)

\widetilde{P}^{\square}:=\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\widehat{\boxtimes}_{\mathcal{O}}{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{tw}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket,

equipped with the diagonal right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket
-linear
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action (where
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on
{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{tw}}
through the determinant). Then
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
carries a natural
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action from the left that commutes with the right
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action. Moreover,
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
does not depend on the choice of
\zeta
.

(3)

There exists
x\in\mathfrak{m}_{R^{\zeta}_{\bar{r}_{p}}}\backslash\big(\mathfrak{m}^{2}_{R^{\zeta}_{\bar{r}_{p}}}+(\varpi)\big)
such that
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
is isomorphic to the projective envelope of
\Sym^{a}\mathbb{F}^{\oplus 2}\otimes\mathrm{det}^{b}
as a right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,x,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module.

Proof.

(1) is
[
Pa13
, Corollary 8.7]
. For (2), the left
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action comes from the isomorphism
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\zeta}\cong R_{\pi_{1}}^{\zeta}
proved in (1). The uniqueness follows from (
7.14.1
).

We now prove (3). For
A=\mathcal{O}
or
\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket
, let
\mathrm{Mod}_{A\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket,\zeta}^{\mathrm{fg}}
denote the category of finitely generated right
A\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-modules with the scalar
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acting by
\zeta
.
By
[
Pa15
, Theorem 5.2]
, there exists
x\in\mathfrak{m}_{R^{\zeta}_{\bar{r}_{p}}}
such that
x:\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\to\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}
is injective and
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}/x\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}
is the projective envelope of
(\mathrm{soc}_{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\bar{\pi}_{1})^{\vee}=\Sym^{a}\mathbb{F}^{\oplus 2}\otimes\mathrm{det}^{b}
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket,\zeta}^{\mathrm{fg}}
. In addition,
[
HP19
, Theorem 3.3(iii)]
proves that
x\notin\big(\mathfrak{m}^{2}_{R^{\zeta}_{\bar{r}_{p}}}+(\varpi)\big)
.
It then remains to show that
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}
is projective in the
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket,\zeta}^{\mathrm{fg}}
, as the projectivity is preserved for tensor products of the form in (
7.15.1
). (Note that the variable
v
in
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}
measuring the central twist of
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
is “absorbed” into the projective envelope as an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module.)
Choose a character
\eta
of
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
such that
\zeta|_{(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}=\eta^{2}
. Then it is enough to show that
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\otimes\eta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{det}
is a projective right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket\llbracket H\rrbracket
-module with
H=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, or equivalently,

\Tor_{>0}^{\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket\llbracket H\rrbracket}(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\otimes\eta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{det},\ \mathbb{F})=0.

But this follows immediately from the spectral sequence

E^{2}_{\bullet,\bullet}=\Tor_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{O}\llbracket H\rrbracket}\Big(\Tor_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket\llbracket H\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\otimes\eta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{det},\ \mathcal{O}\llbracket H\rrbracket\big),\ \mathbb{F}\Big)\ \Rightarrow\ \Tor_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{O}\llbracket x\rrbracket\llbracket H\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\otimes\eta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{det},\ \mathbb{F}\big)

and the properties of
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}/x\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}
above.
∎

Remark 7.16
.

(1)

It is proved in
[
CEGGPS18
, Theorem 6.18]
that
\widetilde{P}_{\zeta}\widehat{\boxtimes}_{\mathcal{O}}{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{tw}}
is isomorphic to the projective envelope of
\pi_{1}^{\vee}
in
\mathfrak{C}
.

(2)

It is tempting to use the “less heavy” tool of patched completed homology of Caraiani–Emerton–Gee–Geraghty–Paškūnas–Shin in
[
CEGGPS16
]
and the globalization process therein, to reproduce the above construction instead of using the Paškūnas module. Unfortunately, we do not know how to implement this idea. The main difficulty is that, while
[
CEGGPS16
]
provides a “minimal patching” in the sense that the patched module is of rank
1
over the patched version of the local Galois deformation ring
R_{\infty}[1/p]
, to invoke our local ghost Theorem
2.8
, we need the patched completed homology to be the projective envelope as an
S_{\infty}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module of a Serre weight. So we would need a certain mod-
p
-multiplicity-one assumption that compares
S_{\infty}
with
R_{\infty}
, which does not seem to be available.

7.17.
Comparison with trianguline deformation space

Continue to consider the
\bar{r}_{p}
as above.
We apply Emerton’s locally analytic Jacquet functor
[
Em06
]
to
\widetilde{P}^{\square}\in\mathfrak{C}
and compare it with the trianguline deformation space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. In a nutshell, we will prove that the reduced eigenvariety
\Eig(\widetilde{P}^{\square})^{\mathrm{red}}
associated to
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
and the
U_{p}
-action on
\Eig(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
corresponds to the universal character
\delta_{2}(p)^{-1}
on
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
.

We first recall the formal part of the construction from
[
BHS17
, § 3]
and
[
BD20
, § A.4]
. Write
S^{\square}:=\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,x,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket
, viewed as a natural subring of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
, which induces a morphism

\mathrm{pr}^{\square}:\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\to\mathcal{S}^{\square}:=\Spf(S^{\square})^{\mathrm{rig}}.

Consider the Schikhof dual of
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
:

\Pi^{\square}:=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{cont}}\big(\widetilde{P}^{\square},E\big).

Applying the locally analytic Jacquet functor construction of Emerton
[
Em06
]
, we obtain

(7.17.1)

\mathcal{M}^{\square}:=\mathrm{swap}^{*}\big(J_{\bar{B}}\big((\Pi^{\square})^{S^{\square}\textrm{-an}}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\big)\cong\mathrm{swap}^{*}\big(J_{\bar{B}}\big((\Pi^{\square})^{R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\textrm{-an}}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\big),

which may be
viewed as a coherent sheaf
over the Stein space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
that further induces a
coherent
sheaf
\mathrm{pr}^{\square}_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\square}
over
\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
(where
\mathcal{T}=(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}})^{2}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
is defined in (
7.3.1
)). Here,

•

(\Pi^{\square})^{R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\textrm{-an}}\subseteq(\Pi^{\square})^{S^{\square}\textrm{-an}}
are respectively locally
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-analytic and
S^{\square}
-analytic vectors as defined in
[
BHS17
, Définition 3.2]
, and they are equal by
[
BHS17
, Proposition 3.8]
as
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
is finitely generated over
S^{\square}\llbracket\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
;

•

J_{\bar{B}}(-)
is the locally analytic Jacquet functor of Emerton defined in
[
Em06
]
(with respect to the lower triangular matrices to match our computation with the setup in §
2.4
, which further agrees with
[
Bu07
]
);

•

(-)^{\prime}_{b}
is the strong dual for Fréchet spaces; and

•

\mathrm{swap}:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{T}
is the morphism swapping two factors, i.e. sending
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\mapsto(\delta_{2},\delta_{1})
. (This is inserted because we used the locally analytic Jacquet functor relative to the lower triangular Borel subgroup, in contrast to
[
BHS17
]
and
[
BD20
]
where the upper triangular Borel subgroup are used.)

Theorem 7.18
.

Let
\Eig(P^{\square})
denote the schematic support of
\mathcal{M}^{\square}
over
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
.

(1)

The space
\Eig(P^{\square})
is contained in the subspace of
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
consisting of points
(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
for which
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}_{x})
corresponds to
\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
under the local class field theory.

(2)

The reduced subscheme of
\Eig(P^{\square})
is precisely the trianguline deformation space
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
(Definition
7.4
).

Proof.

(1) is clear because (if
\zeta(p)=\zeta(1+p)=1
), the right actions of
{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p&0\\
0&p\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
and the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
are precisely given on
{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{tw}}
, which agrees with the
\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,v\rrbracket
-action as described just before Theorem
7.15
.

(2) is proved at the beginning of
[
BD20
, Page 134]
(except that we have the framing variables, and we used the lower triangular Borel subgroup for the locally analytic Jacquet functor). We summarize the gist for the benefit of the readers.

At an
E^{\prime}
-point
x\in(\mathcal{V}_{x},\delta_{1,x},\delta_{2,x})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}
, let
\mathfrak{p}_{x}\subseteq R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
be the corresponding prime ideal.
Then
\Pi^{\square}[\mathfrak{p}_{x}]=\pi(\mathcal{V}_{x})
is the
p
-adic Banach space representation over
E^{\prime}
attached to
\mathcal{V}_{x}
. So
x
lies in
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
if and only if there is a
(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times})^{2}
-embedding

\delta_{2,x}\times\delta_{1,x}\hookrightarrow J_{\bar{B}}\big(\Pi^{\square,R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\textrm{-an}}[\mathfrak{p}_{x}]\big)=J_{\bar{B}}(\pi(\mathcal{V}_{x})^{\mathrm{an}}).

(Note that, comparing to
[
BD20
]
where
J_{B}(-)
is used, the lower triangular locally analytic Jacquet functor has the effect of “swapping” two factors.)
By the description of locally analytic vectors for
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence
[
Colm10a
,
LXZ12
]
(and the full power of
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence), there is an embedding
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}\hookrightarrow\Eig(P^{\square})
. Applying a typical construction of eigenvarieties shows that points in
\mathcal{U}_{\bar{r}_{p},\mathrm{reg}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
are also Zariski-dense and accumulating in
\Eig(P^{\square})
. This completes the proof of that
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
is isomorphic to the reduced subscheme of
\Eig(P^{\square})
.
∎

Remark 7.19
.

In fact, one can prove that, in our case,
\Eig(P^{\square})=\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
.

7.20.
Relating locally analytic Jacquet functor with local ghost theorem I

We will deduce Theorem
7.6
by applying local ghost Theorem
2.8
to
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
with all possible evaluations of the formal variables
u,x,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
. For this, we need an intermediate step to relate the characteristic power series of abstract
p
-adic forms in the local ghost theorem with the abstract construction of eigenvarieties in §
7.17
.
This is essentially explained in
[
Em06
, Proposition 4.2.36]
: one may compute the locally analytic Jacquet functor when
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
is a finite projective
S^{\square}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-module, using the eigenvariety machine of Buzzard.

Let
\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{N}}
denote the
right
ideal of
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{Iw}_{p}\rrbracket
generated by
\big[{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
p&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\big]-1
; then by Iwasawa decomposition, we may write

(7.20.1)

\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{Iw}_{p}\rrbracket/\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{N}}\cong\mathcal{D}_{0}\Big({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)};\,\mathcal{O}\Big\llbracket\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
&\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\Big\rrbracket\Big)=\mathcal{D}_{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\,\mathcal{O}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket\big),

where the
\mathcal{D}_{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};-)
is the space of measures on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, dual to
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p};-)
. Here the induced left
\mathrm{Iw}_{p}
-action on the right hand side of (
7.20.1
) extends to an action of
\mathbf{M}_{1}=\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
p\mathbb{Z}_{p}&\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)^{\mathrm{det}\neq 0}
given by, for
{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\in\mathbf{M}_{1}
with
\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma=p^{r}d
for
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,

\big\langle{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\cdot\mu,\,h(z)\big\rangle=\Big\langle\mu,\Big[\Big(\frac{d}{\gamma z+\delta},\,\gamma z+\delta\Big)\Big]\cdot h\Big(\frac{\alpha z+\beta}{\gamma z+\delta}\Big)\Big\rangle.

(After tensored with
