\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}
,) this is precisely dual to the right
\mathbf{M}_{1}
-action on
\mathcal{C}^{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}\big)
given by (
2.4.4
). We define the
abstract
p
-adic distribution
associated to
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
to be

\mathrm{S}^{\vee}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}}:=\widetilde{P}^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{Iw}_{p}\rrbracket}\mathcal{D}_{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\,\mathcal{O}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket\big),

equipped with the infinite product topology (which is automatically
compact
). Then we have a tautological isomorphism (from the tensor-hom adjunction)

(7.20.2)

\Hom_{S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket}\big(\mathrm{S}^{\vee}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}},S^{\square}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}\big)\cong\Hom_{S^{\square}\llbracket\mathrm{Iw}_{p}\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{P}^{\square},\,\mathcal{C}^{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\,S^{\square}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}\big)\big).

Define an
S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket
-linear operator
U_{p}^{\vee}
on
\mathrm{S}^{\vee}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}}

given by (choosing a coset decomposition
\mathrm{Iw}_{p}{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\mathrm{Iw}_{p}=\coprod_{j=0}^{p-1}v_{j}\mathrm{Iw}_{p}
, e.g.
v_{j}={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&0\\
j&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
and
v_{j}^{-1}={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p&0\\
-jp&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
),

U_{p}^{\vee}(x\otimes\mu):=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}xv_{j}\otimes v_{j}^{-1}\mu\qquad\textrm{for }x\in\widetilde{P}^{\square}\textrm{ and }\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\,\mathcal{O}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket\big).

Applying an argument similar to
[
LTXZ

24

, § 2.10]
(or essentially Buzzard’s original eigenvarieties machine in
[
Bu07
]
), we may define a characteristic power series for the
S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket
-linear
U_{p}^{\vee}
-action on
\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}}^{\vee}
:

C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}}(t)=1+c_{1}t+c_{2}t^{2}+\cdots\in S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket\llbracket t\rrbracket.

Let
\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
denote the hypersurface of
\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}
cut out by
C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}}(t)
. Then the general Buzzard’s eigenvariety machine of
[
Bu07
]
outputs a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{N}^{\square}
on
\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
corresponding to finite slope forms in
\mathrm{S}^{\vee}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}}
.
On the other hand, the left
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action on
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
(extending the
S^{\square}
-action) induces an action of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
on the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{N}^{\square}
. Let
\widetilde{\Eig}^{\prime}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
denote the rigid analytic space over
\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
associated to the image of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
in the endomorphism algebra
\End_{\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})}(\mathcal{N}^{\square})
; then we may “upgrade”
\mathcal{N}^{\square}
to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}^{\square\prime}
on
\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
whose pushforward along
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\to\mathcal{S}^{\square}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{N}^{\square}
.
The following diagram summarizes the above construction.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\square\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Eig}^{\prime}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}^{\square}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}

In fact,
\mathcal{M}^{\square\prime}
is essentially the same as
\mathcal{M}^{\square}
of (
7.17.1
) in the following sense.
By Theorem
7.18
(1),
\mathcal{M}^{\square}
is supported on the subspace

(7.20.3)

\mathcal{Z}=\big\{(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}\;\big|\;\mathrm{det}\mathcal{V}_{x}(p)=\delta_{1}(p)\delta_{2}(p)\big\}.

The natural map

(7.20.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(x,\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}},\delta_{1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}},\delta_{2}(p)\big)}

induces an isomorphism
\iota:\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}
. Then
\iota^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\square\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}^{\square}
; in particular, the reduced subscheme of
\widetilde{\Eig}^{\prime}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
is precisely
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
by Theorem
7.18
. Here we point out three subtleties in normalizations:

(1)

The
U_{p}^{\vee}
-operator is associated to the double coset
\mathrm{Iw}_{p}{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\mathrm{Iw}_{p}
, and the zeros of
C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}}(t)
gives the
reciprocal
of
U_{p}^{\vee}
-eigenvalues;

(2)

the swapping of
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
is caused by taking
J_{\bar{B}}(-)
as opposed to
J_{B}(-)
; and

(3)

the additional twist of cyclotomic character is built-in for the theory of locally analytic Jacquet functors.

7.21.
Relating locally analytic Jacquet functor with local ghost theorem II

It remains to relate
C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}}(t)
and the slopes appearing in
the local ghost Theorem
2.8
.
For each homomorphism
y^{*}:S^{\square}=\mathcal{O}\llbracket u,x,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
, write
\widetilde{P}_{y}:=\widetilde{P}^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S^{\square},y^{*}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. Then Theorem
7.15
(3) implies that
\widetilde{P}_{y}
is a primitive
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective augmented module of type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
(the Serre weight determined in Notation
7.2
), where the conditions (2) and (3) of Definition
2.2
are clear from (
7.15.1
).

For a character
\varepsilon
of
\Delta^{2}
relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, recall that there is a natural quotient map

(7.21.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{*}:\mathcal{O}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\alpha,\delta]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon(\bar{\alpha},\bar{\delta})(1+w)^{\log(\delta/\omega(\bar{\delta}))/p}}

for
\alpha,\delta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. This quotient map is a twist of (
7.1.1
).
The homomorphism (
7.21.1
) together with
y^{*}
defines an embedding

y\otimes\varepsilon:\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}.

The isomorphism (
7.20.2
) then induces a canonical
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket
-linear isomorphism

(7.21.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{S}^{\vee}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square},p\textrm{-adic}}\otimes_{S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket,(y\otimes\varepsilon)^{*}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}}\big(\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y},p\textrm{-adic}}^{(\varepsilon)},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}\big),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

which can be expressed in terms of a pairing: for
x\in\widetilde{P}^{\square}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{0}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{p};\,\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}\big)
, and
\varphi\in\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y},p\textrm{-adic}}^{(\varepsilon)}
,

\big\langle\varphi,x\otimes\mu\big\rangle:=\langle\varphi(x),\mu\rangle.

We deduce the compatibility of
U_{p}^{\vee}
-operator on the left hand side of (
7.21.2
) and the dual of
U_{p}
-action on the right hand side easily as: with the notation as above and
v_{j}={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&0\\
j&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
for
j=0,\dots,p-1
,

\displaystyle\langle U_{p}(\varphi),x\otimes\mu\rangle=\

\displaystyle\langle U_{p}(\varphi)(x),\mu\rangle=\Big\langle\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\varphi(xv_{j})|_{v_{j}^{-1}},\,\mu\Big\rangle=\Big\langle\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\varphi(xv_{j}),\,v_{j}^{-1}\mu\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\Big\langle\varphi,\,\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}xv_{j}\otimes v_{j}^{-1}\mu\Big\rangle=\langle\varphi,\,U_{p}^{\vee}(x\otimes\mu)\rangle.

This in particular means that, under the map
(y\otimes\varepsilon)^{*}:S^{\square}\llbracket(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{(\varepsilon)}
, we have an identity of characteristic power series:

(7.21.3)

(y\otimes\varepsilon)^{*}\big(C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}}(t)\big)=C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t).

Writing
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y})
for the zero locus of
C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
inside
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}
. Then
(y\otimes\varepsilon)^{-1}\big(\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})\big)=\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y})
.

7.22.
Proof of Theorem
7.6

Now, we conclude the proof of Theorem
7.6
. By the discussion in §
7.11
, we may assume that
\bar{r}_{p}
is reducible nonsplit and very generic with
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
and
b=0
.
Let
\underline{x}=(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
be an
E^{\prime}
-point;
set
w_{\star}:=(\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})(\exp(p))-1
and
\varepsilon=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}
, which is relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
as already shown in §
7.11
.
we need to show that
-v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))
is equal to a slope appearing in
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
.

The argument is summarized by the following diagram:

(7.22.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\!\!\!\underline{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Supp}(\mathrm{pr}_{*}^{\square}\mathcal{M}^{\square})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\!\!\!\!\!x\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{y\}\times\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\!\!\!\!\!y\in\mathcal{S}^{\square}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{2}(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{2}(p)}
(
7.20.4
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{W}\textrm{ of }\eqref{E:W vs tilde W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y\otimes\varepsilon}

By Proposition
7.5
(5), we may assume that
\delta_{2}|_{(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}}
is trivial.
Write
y
for the image of
\underline{x}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\square}
and let
y^{*}:S^{\square}\to E^{\prime}
be the induced map.
Then the image of
\underline{x}
in
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathrm{pr}_{*}^{\square}\mathcal{M}^{\square})
is precisely given by
(y,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
. In particular, the map taking the value of
\delta_{2}(p)
on
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
factors through
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathrm{pr}_{*}^{\square}\mathcal{M}^{\square})
.

As explained in §
7.20
, the image of
\underline{x}
in
\widetilde{\Spc}(\widetilde{P}^{\square})
admits a cyclotomic twist from (
7.20.4
); so it is
\underline{x}^{\prime}:=(y,\delta_{2},\delta_{1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1})
. In particular, the image of
\underline{x}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\square}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
is precisely
y\otimes\varepsilon(w_{\star})
with
w_{\star}=\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}(\exp(p))-1
and
\varepsilon=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}
. So
v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))
at
\underline{x}^{\prime}
can be seen on
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{P}_{y}^{\square})
. By local ghost Theorem
2.8
,
-v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))
is a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
. Theorem
7.6
except (3) is proved.

For Theorem
7.6
(3), we may twist the point
x
so that
\delta_{1}(p)\delta_{2}(p)=1
; this translate to that
{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p&0\\
0&p\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{P}^{\square}
.
As argued above, it suffices to show that for the given
k
, all slopes
\frac{k-2}{2}
appearing in
\NP\big(C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k},-)\big)
(with multiplicity
d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}(\varepsilon_{1})
by Proposition
2.17
and Theorem
2.8
) genuinely come from the zeros
\pm p^{-(k-2)/2}
of
C_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k},-)
.
Indeed, by Corollary
3.7
, the multiplicities of
U_{p}
-eigenvalues
\pm p^{-(k-2)/2}
on
\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{P}^{\square}_{y},k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})
are
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{new}}(\varepsilon_{1})
each. Theorem
7.6
(3) is proved.

Finally, we remark that “conversely” part of Theorem
7.6
is also clear from the above discussion: given any
\delta_{1}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
and
\delta_{2}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
with
\varepsilon
and
w_{\star}
defined therein. We can pick an
arbitrary
evaluation
y^{*}:S^{\square}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. Then there exists a point
\tilde{x}\in\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{P}_{y}^{\square})
with any given slope of
\NP(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-))
by local ghost theorem (Theorem
2.8
). This then produces a point in
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathrm{pr}_{*}^{\square}\mathcal{M}^{\square})
in the commutative diagram (
7.22.1
) which can then be lifted to a desired point
\underline{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
7.6
.

8.
Bootstrapping and ghost conjecture

In this section, we perform a bootstrapping argument to prove a global ghost conjecture (Theorem
8.7
) when the residual Galois representation
\bar{r}
is absolutely irreducible yet its restriction to
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is reducible and very generic (
2\leq a\leq p-5
and
p\geq 11
). The global ghost conjecture implies the following (with the help of
[
BP19b
]
and
[
Re

24

]
) for the
\bar{r}
-localized space of modular forms:

•

a version of the Gouvêa–Mazur conjecture (Theorem
8.10
),

•

Gouvêa’s conjecture on slope distributions (Theorem
8.11
), and

•

a refined version of Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard–Kilford spectral halo conjecture (Theorem
8.12
).

In fact, we adopt an axiomatic approach to proving the global ghost conjecture, borrowing a setup from
[
CEGGPS18
]
,
[
GN22
, § 5]
, and
[
DoLe21
, § 4.2]
; this allows our theorem to be applicable to the cohomology of general Shimura varieties associated to a group
G
which is essentially
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
at a
p
-adic place.

In this section, let
\bar{r}_{p}
be a residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
. Let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
be as in Notation
7.2
.

8.1.
Hecke actions

Instead of developing the theory of Hecke actions for general
\mathrm{K}_{p}
-types as in
[
CEGGPS16
, § 4]
, we focus on the simplest spherical case.

Recall that for a
\mathrm{K}_{p}
-projective augmented module
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
, a character
\varepsilon_{1}
of
\Delta
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, the space
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathcal{O}[z]^{\leq k-2}\otimes\varepsilon_{1}\circ\mathrm{det}\big)
carries a
T_{p}
-operator as defined in §
2.4
(4).
We similarly define an operator
S_{p}
on
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
given by, for
\varphi\in\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
and
x\in\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
,

S_{p}(\varphi)(x)=\varphi\big(x{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&0\\
0&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\big).

The action of
S_{p}
is invertible and commutes with the
T_{p}
-operator. So
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
admits a
\mathcal{O}[T_{p},S_{p}^{\pm 1}]
-module structure.

Recall the associated Kisin’s crystabelline deformation ring from §
7.7
.
Let
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}
be the quotient of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
parameterizing crystabelline representations with Hodge–Tate weights
\{1-k,0\}
such that
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(-)
by
\varepsilon_{1}
(see Notation
7.1
for the definition of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(-)
).
Let
\mathcal{V}_{1-k}
denote the universal representation on
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}:=\big(\Spf R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}
, then
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{pcrys}}(\mathcal{V}_{1-k})
is locally free of rank two over
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}
, equipped with a linear action of crystalline Frobenius
\phi
. In particular, our condition says that
\mathcal{V}_{1-k}\otimes\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}
is crystalline.

Define elements
s_{p}\in\mathcal{O}\big(\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big)^{\times}
and
t_{p}\in\mathcal{O}\big(\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big)
such that

\mathrm{det}(\phi^{-1})=p^{k-1}s_{p}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathrm{tr}(\phi^{-1})=t_{p}.

Here we considered the trace of
\phi^{-1}
because our associated Galois representation is the one that matches with the local Langlands correspondence of Harris–Taylor; see §
1.28
for details.
As both
s_{p}
and
t_{p}
take bounded values, we have
s_{p}\in R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big[\frac{1}{p}\big]^{\times}
and
t_{p}\in R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big[\frac{1}{p}\big]
.

Following
[
CEGGPS16
, § 4]
, we define a natural homomorphism

(8.1.1)

\eta_{k}:\mathcal{O}[T_{p},S_{p}^{\pm 1}]\to R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big[\tfrac{1}{p}\big]\quad\textrm{given by}\quad\eta_{k}(T_{p})=t_{p},\textrm{ and }\eta_{k}(S_{p})=s_{p}.

Definition 8.2
.

Recall
\mathrm{K}_{p}=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and the representation
\bar{r}_{p}
from Notation
7.2
.
For a Serre weight
\sigma_{a,b}
, write
\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})
for the projective envelope of
\sigma_{a,b}
as an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-module.

An
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}
is an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective augmented module
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
equipped with a continuous
left
action of
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

The left
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
commutes with the right
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action.

(2)

The induced
\mathrm{K}_{p}
-action makes
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
a right
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-module isomorphic to

•

\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})^{\oplus m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}
for some
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, if
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit, or

•

\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})^{\oplus m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}\oplus\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{p-3-a,a+b+1})^{\oplus m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}
for some
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}),m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, if
\bar{r}_{p}
is split (writing
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}):=m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})+m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
in this case).

(3)

For every character
\varepsilon=\omega^{-s_{\varepsilon}+b}\times\omega^{a+s_{\varepsilon}+b}
relevant to
\sigma_{a,b}
and every
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
, the induced
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action on
\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
factors through the quotient
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}
. Moreover, the Hecke action of
\mathcal{O}[T_{p},S_{p}^{\pm 1}]
on
\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
defined in §
8.1
agrees with the composition

\mathcal{O}[T_{p},S_{p}^{\pm 1}]\xrightarrow{\eqref{E:eta}}R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\big[\tfrac{1}{p}\big]\to\End_{E}\big(\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E\big).

When
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit, we say that
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
is
primitive
if
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=1
.

In either case, we call
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
the
multiplicity
of
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
.

Remark 8.3
.

(1)

In applications, all the
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic modules we encounter are known to satisfy conditions analogous to Definition
8.2
(3) for all
crystabelline
representations. (Such compatibility can be alternatively deduced by comparing to trianguline deformations.) But formulating of such condition is slightly more subtle; we refer to for example
[
CEGGPS18
, Definition 1.5]
or
[
DoLe21
, § 4.2]
.

(2)

Our definition is essentially different from and (in most cases) weaker than the notion of
\mathcal{O}[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})]
-modules
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
with arithmetic actions (see for example,
[
CEGGPS18
,
GN22
,
DoLe21
]
) in the following aspects: (a) their
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is a module of
R_{\infty}=R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\llbracket z_{1},\dots,z_{g}\rrbracket
for some dummy variables; ours
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
may be viewed as
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
after evaluating
z_{i}
’s; (b) they typically require
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}\widehat{\otimes}\Sym^{k-2}\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}
to be a maximal Cohen–Macaulay over
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}}\llbracket z_{1},\dots,z_{g}\rrbracket
; we do not need this.

(3)

When
\bar{r}_{p}
is split, it may happen in practice that
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\neq m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
.

(4)

We do not require primitive
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic modules to satisfy the two additional conditions in Definition
2.2
(2)(3), despite they typically do in practice.

Example 8.4

(Quaternionic case)
.

We illustrate by an example how our abstract setup appears naturally in the study of cohomology of Shimura varieties.

Fix an absolutely irreducible residual Galois representation
\bar{r}:\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
such that
\bar{r}|_{\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\simeq\bar{r}_{p}
for a residual local representation that we consider in Notation
7.2
.
Let
D
be a quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
that is unramified at
p
; we fix an isomorphism
D\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cong\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Set

i(D):=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{ if }D\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}\cong\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\textrm{ which we call the \emph{indefinite} case};\\
0&\textrm{ if }D\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}\cong\mathbb{H},\textrm{ which we call the \emph{definite} case}.\end{cases}

Fix an open compact subgroup
K^{p}\subseteq(D\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})^{\times}
such that
K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p}
is
neat
, i.e.
gD^{\times}g^{-1}\cap K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p}=\{1\}
for every
g\in(D\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f})^{\times}
.
For any open compact subgroup
K^{\prime}_{p}\subseteq\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, let
\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p})
denote the associated (complex) Shimura variety, with
\mathbb{C}
-points given by

\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p})(\mathbb{C})=\begin{cases}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f})^{\times}/K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}&\textrm{ when }i(D)=0\\
D^{\times}\backslash\mathfrak{H}^{\pm}\times(D\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f})^{\times}/K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}&\textrm{ when }i(D)=1,\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{H}^{\pm}:=\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{R}
. (When
i(D)=1
, we take the Deligne homomorphisms to be the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy of
h:\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R})\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{R})
given by
h(x+\mathtt{i}y)={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}x&y\\
-y&x\end{smallmatrix}\big)}
.)
Then for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, the tower of subgroups
\mathrm{K}_{p,n}:=\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\subseteq\mathrm{K}_{p}
defines a tower of Shimura varieties:

\cdots\to\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p,n})\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p,1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p}).

The
i(D)
th completed homology group localized at
\bar{r}

\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}:=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}_{i(D)}^{\mathrm{Betti}}\big(\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p,n})(\mathbb{C}),\mathcal{O}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}^{\mathrm{cplx}=1},

where the subscript
\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}
indicates localization at the maximal Hecke ideal at
\bar{r}
, and the superscript cplx=
1
is meaningless when
i(D)=0
, and means to take the subspace where the complex conjugation acts by
1
(so that we only take a one-dimensional subspace of the associated
2
-dimensional Galois representation).

This
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
is a
\mathrm{K}_{p}
-projective augmented module. Indeed, this is obvious if
i(D)=0
; when
i(D)=1
, this is because, for any open compact subgroup
K^{\prime}_{p}\subseteq\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the localization

(8.4.1)

\mathrm{H}_{i}^{\mathrm{Betti}}\big(\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p})(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{F}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}=0\textrm{ unless }i=1,

and the projectivity of
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
follows from studying the usual Tor-spectral sequence. Moreover,
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
carries an action of
R_{\bar{r}}
, the Galois deformation ring of
\bar{r}
. To make this compatible with our setup of Definition
8.2
, we choose an isomorphism
R_{\bar{r}}^{\square}\cong R_{\bar{r}}\llbracket y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\rrbracket
and demand that
y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}
act trivially on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
. This then induces a natural
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
, upgrading
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}}
to an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}
, where the condition Definition
8.2
(3) is the usual local-global compatibility of automorphic forms on
D^{\times}
.

In this case, the spaces of abstract classical forms defined in §
2.4
(3) recover the usual étale cohomology groups: for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and characters
\varepsilon_{1}
of
\Delta
and
\psi
of
\Delta^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}},k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})\,

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}},\,E[z]^{\leq k-2}\otimes\varepsilon_{1}\circ\mathrm{det}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{i(D)}_{\mathrm{Betti}}\big(\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p})(\mathbb{C}),\,\Sym^{k-2}\mathcal{H}\otimes\varepsilon_{1}\circ\mathrm{det}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}^{\mathrm{cplx}=1}\cong\big(\mathrm{S}^{D}_{k}(K^{p}\mathrm{K}_{p})\otimes\varepsilon_{1}\circ\mathrm{det}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}},k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\psi)\,

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{Iw}_{p}\rrbracket}\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\bar{r}},\,E[z]^{\leq k-2}\otimes\psi\big)

\displaystyle\cong\

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{i(D)}_{\mathrm{Betti}}\big(\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}\mathrm{Iw}_{p})(\mathbb{C}),\,\Sym^{k-2}\mathcal{H}\otimes\psi\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}^{\mathrm{cplx}=1}\cong\mathrm{S}^{D}_{k}(K^{p}\mathrm{Iw}_{p};\psi)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}.

Here
\mathcal{H}
is the usual rank
2
local system on
\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}(K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p})
associated to the dual of standard representation of
K^{\prime}_{p}\subset\mathrm{K}_{p}
(and
\psi
also makes use of the local system
\mathcal{H}
as opposed to the relative Tate modules);
S^{D}_{k}(-)
denotes the space of automorphic forms on
\mathrm{Sh}_{D^{\times}}
, and the isomorphisms are as Hecke modules. This example allows us to deduce results regarding classical modular forms or quaternionic automorphic forms from our abstract setup.

Remark 8.5
.

Similar constructions can be made for Shimura varieties associated to a more general group
G
for which
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
admits a factor isomorphic to
\mathrm{PGL}_{2,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(after properly treating the central characters), as long as one can prove certain vanishing result similar to (
8.4.1
). (Such techniques are available for example in
[
CS

17

]
.)

Example 8.6

(Patched version)
.

Another source of

\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic modules is the patched completed homology of Caraiani–Emerton–Gee–Geraghty–Paškūnas–Shin in
[
CEGGPS16
]
. More precisely, let
\mathcal{G}_{2}
be the group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
defined in
[
CHT08
, § 2.1]
, which contains
\GL_{2}\times\GL_{1}
as a subgroup of index
2
, and admits a natural homomorphism
\nu:\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\GL_{1}
.
Let
F
be a CM field with maximal totally real subfield
F^{+}
,
\bar{r}:\Gal_{F^{+}}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}(\mathbb{F})
a residual global representation, and
G
a definite unitary group over
F^{+}
satisfying the following list of properties:

(1)

\bar{r}^{-1}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})\times\mathbb{F}^{\times})=\Gal_{F}
, and write
\bar{r}|_{\Gal_{F}}
for the representation
\bar{r}:\Gal_{F}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})\times\mathbb{F}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
;

(2)

\nu\circ\bar{r}=\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{-1}
, where
\bar{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl}}
is the reduction of the cyclotomic character;

(3)

there is a
p
-adic place
\mathfrak{p}
of
F^{+}
which splits into
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
in
F
such that
F_{\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}}\cong F^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\bar{r}|_{\Gal_{F_{\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\cong\bar{r}_{p}
, for the
\bar{r}_{p}
we consider in Notation
7.2
;

(4)

\bar{r}(\Gal_{F(\zeta_{p})})
is adequate in the sense of
[
Th12
, Definition 2.3]
; in particular,
\bar{r}
is irreducible;

(5)

\overline{F}^{\ker\mathrm{ad}\bar{r}|_{\Gal_{F}}}
does not contain
F(\zeta_{p})
.

(6)

G
is an outer form of
\GL_{2}
with
G\times_{F^{+}}F\cong\GL_{2,F}
;

(7)

if
v
is a finite place of
F^{+}
, then
G
is quasi-split at
v
;

(8)

if
v
is an infinite place of
F^{+}
, then
G(F^{+}_{v})\cong U_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and

(9)

\bar{r}
is automorphic in the sense of
[
EG14
, Definition 5.3.1]
.

Fix an isomorphism
G(\mathcal{O}_{F^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}})\cong\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\mathrm{K}_{p}
, and fix a neat open compact subgroup
K^{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}_{F^{+},f}^{(\mathfrak{p})})
. As above, consider the subgroups
\mathrm{K}_{p,n}:=\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\subseteq\mathrm{K}_{p}
for each
n
.
With these global data,
[
CEGGPS16
]
constructed a patched completed homology
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
, that patches the usual completed homology

\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{0}\big(G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\mathfrak{p}},\mathcal{O}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}:=\varprojlim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{H}_{0}\big(G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{K}_{p,n},\mathcal{O}\big)_{\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}},

where
\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}
is the appropriate Hecke maximal ideal associated to
\bar{r}
.
The additional structure associated to
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
is explained by the following diagram

(8.6.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{n}R_{\bar{r},\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{\square}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y^{*}}

•

S_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}\llbracket z_{1},\dots,z_{h}\rrbracket
is the ring of formal power series formed by patching variables and framing variables;

•

\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
is a projective right
S_{\infty}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-module isomorphic to

–

\mathrm{Proj}_{S_{\infty}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})^{\oplus m(\bar{r})}
for some
m(\bar{r})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, if
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit, or

–

\mathrm{Proj}_{S_{\infty}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})^{\oplus m^{\prime}(\bar{r})}\oplus\mathrm{Proj}_{S_{\infty}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{p-3-a,a+b+1})^{\oplus m^{\prime\prime}(\bar{r})}
for some
m^{\prime}(\bar{r}),m^{\prime\prime}(\bar{r})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, if
\bar{r}_{p}
is split;

•

the right
\mathrm{K}_{p}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
extends to a continuous right
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action;

•

the set
\mathsf{Q}_{n}
denotes a collection of Taylor–Wiles primes of level
n
.

•

\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
is essentially constructed as an inverse limit, carrying an action of the inverse limit of deformation rings
R_{\bar{r},\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{\square}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{n}
, which commutes with the right
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action;

•

the action of
S_{\infty}
on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
factors through that of
\varprojlim_{n}R_{\bar{r},\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{\square}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{n}
;

•

the local deformation ring
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
naturally maps to
\varprojlim_{n}R_{\bar{r},\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{\square}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{n}
and acts on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}
;

•

one may lift the homomorphism
S_{\infty}\to\varprojlim_{n}R_{\bar{r},\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{\square}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{Q}_{n}}^{n}
to a homomorphism to
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
(somewhat arbitrarily).

A main result of
[
CEGGPS16
, Theorem 4.1]
says that, for any homomorphism
y^{*}:S_{\infty}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
,
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{y}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{\infty,\mathfrak{m}_{\bar{r}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{\infty}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
carries naturally a structure of
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}\cdot\omega_{1}
in the sense of Definition
8.2
by verifying the local-global compatibility condition (3). (The additional twist by cyclotomic character is due to the different half twist from local Langlands correspondence.)

Recall the residual representations
\bar{r}_{p}
from Notation
7.2
.
The main theorem of this paper is the following.

Theorem 8.7
.

Assume that
p\geq 11
.
Let
\bar{r}_{p}
be a residual local Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
be an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}
and multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
in the sense of Definition
8.2
. Fix a character
\varepsilon
of
\Delta^{2}
relevant to
\sigma_{a,b}
. Let
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
denote the characteristic power series for the
U_{p}
-action on the space of abstract
p
-adic forms associated to
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
, as defined in §
2.4
(2).

Then for every
w_{\star}\in\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, the Newton polygon
\NP\big(C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is the same as the Newton polygon
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, stretched in both
x
- and
y
-directions by
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
, except that the slope zero part of
\NP\big(C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is changed to

•

have length
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
, and

•

have length
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
.

When
\bar{r}_{p}
is split, the Newton polygon described in Theorem
8.7
is the convex polygon whose slope multiset is the disjoint union of
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
copies of slope multiset of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
and
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
copies of slope multiset of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, by Proposition
2.15
.

In view of Example
8.4
, Theorem
1.3
follows immediately from this theorem.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two steps. We first show that at each point
w_{\star}\in\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, all possible slopes of
\NP\big(C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
are contained in the set of slopes of the Newton polygon of the corresponding ghost series; this comes from “embedding” the eigencurve into the trianguline deformation space (essentially following the standard classicality argument and the global triangulations
[
KPX14
,
Li15
]
). With this at hand, we can “link” together the slopes at various
w_{\star}
to determine the multiplicities of each slope appearing in
\NP\big(C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
.

We fix a character
\varepsilon
relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
(and hence relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
) throughout the entire proof.

Step I:
Let
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
denote the hypersurface in
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}
defined by
C^{(\varepsilon)}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}(w,t)
; it is the
spectral curve
in the sense of
[
Bu07
]
.
Applying the construction of
[
Bu07
, § 5]
to the algebra
R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}[U_{p}]
acting on
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
, we obtain an
eigencurve

\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
over
\Spc_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}
(which also lives over
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}
). The following commutative diagram summarizes the relations between the spectral curve and the eigencurve.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{wt}}

Consider the following natural embedding

(8.7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{(\varepsilon)}:\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,w_{\star},a_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}),}

where
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
are continuous characters of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
uniquely determined by the conditions

•

\delta_{2}(p)=a_{p}^{-1}
,
\delta_{1}(p)\delta_{2}(p)=\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}_{x})(p)
,

•

\delta_{1}(\exp(p))=\exp(p)(1+w_{\star})
,
\delta_{2}(\exp(p))=1
, and

•

\varepsilon=\delta_{2}|_{\Delta}\times\delta_{1}|_{\Delta}\cdot\omega^{-1}
.

We claim that
\iota^{(\varepsilon)}\big(\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})^{\mathrm{red}}\big)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
. This is a standard argument using the density of classical points; we only sketch the argument.

First we prove this for
very classical points
: an
E^{\prime}
-point
\underline{x}=(x,w_{\star},a_{p})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
is called
very classical
if
w_{\star}=w_{k}
with
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
, and if
v_{p}(a_{p})<\frac{k-2}{2}
. For such a point, classicality result Proposition
2.12
(1) shows that the abstract
p
-adic
U_{p}
-eigenform associated to the point
\underline{x}
belongs to
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})
. So condition Definition
8.2
(3) implies that
x
in fact belongs to
\Spf(R_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,1-k,\varepsilon_{1}})^{\mathrm{rig}}
, which further implies that
\mathcal{V}_{x}
is crystalline, and the two characters
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
exactly upgrades it to a point in
\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
, i.e.
\iota^{(\varepsilon)}(\underline{x})\in\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
.

It remains to show that very classical points are Zariski dense in each irreducible component of
\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
. As
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is defined by Fredholm series,
[
Con99
, Theorem 4.2.2]
shows that every irreducible component of
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is defined by a Fredholm series and hence is surjective onto
\mathcal{W}
.
Fix an irreducible component
\mathcal{Z}
of
\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
and pick a point
\underline{x}=(x,w_{k_{\varepsilon}},a_{p})
. There exists an open affinoid neighborhood
U
of
\underline{x}
that maps surjectively to an open neighborhood
\mathrm{wt}(U)
of
w_{k_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
and that
v_{p}(\delta_{2}(p))
is constant on
U
. Then there are infinitely many weights
w_{k}\in\mathrm{wt}(U)
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>2v_{p}(a_{p})+2}
and
k\equiv k_{\varepsilon}\bmod(p-1)
, and each point in
\mathrm{wt}^{-1}(w_{k})\cap U
is a very classical point. This means that very classical points are Zariski dense in
U
and hence in
\mathcal{Z}
. Taking Zariski closure proves that
\iota^{(\varepsilon)}\big(\Eig^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})^{\mathrm{red}}\big)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\bar{r}_{p}}^{\square,\mathrm{tri}}
.

As a corollary of this claim and Theorem
7.6
, for each closed point
\underline{x}=(w_{\star},a_{p})\in\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
,
v_{p}(a_{p})
is always a slope of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, with only one possible exception:
v_{p}(a_{p})=0
,
\bar{r}_{p}
is split, and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
(from Theorem
7.6
(2)). (Recall that
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
only accounts for slopes for the nonsplit
\bar{r}_{p}
.)

Step II:

Write
\mathrm{wt}:\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}\to\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
for the natural weight map.
Recall from Proposition
2.20
(3) that, for each fixed
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, all elements
w_{\star}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
for which
(n,v_{p}(g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star})))
is a vertex of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
form a quasi-Stein open subspace of
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
:

\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}=\bigcup_{\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\,\delta\to 0^{+}}\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}\quad\textrm{with}

\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}:=\Bigg\{w_{\star}\in\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;\Bigg|\;\begin{array}[]{l}v_{p}(w_{\star})\geq\delta,\textrm{ and for each $k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}$}\\
\textrm{such that }n\in\big(d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1}),d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})\big),\textrm{ we have}\\
v_{p}(w_{\star}-w_{k})\leq\Delta^{(\varepsilon)}_{k,|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-n|+1}-\Delta^{(\varepsilon)}_{k,|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-n|}-\delta.\end{array}\Bigg\}.

By the compactness argument in Corollary
2.27
, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
\epsilon_{\delta}\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that for every point
w_{\star}\in\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
, the difference between the left and right slopes at
x=n
of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
is at least
\epsilon_{\delta}
. Thus the following two subspaces are the same:

\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta}:=\bigg\{(w_{\star},a_{p})\in\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\;\bigg|\;\begin{array}[]{l}w_{\star}\in\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)(\mathbb{C}_{p}),\delta},\textrm{ and}\\
-v_{p}(a_{p})\leq\textrm{$n$th slope of }\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)\end{array}\!\bigg\},

\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta,+}:=\bigg\{(w_{\star},a_{p})\in\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\;\bigg|\;\begin{array}[]{l}w_{\star}\in\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)(\mathbb{C}_{p}),\delta},\textrm{ and}\\
-v_{p}(a_{p})\leq\epsilon_{\delta}+\textrm{$n$th slope of }\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)\end{array}\!\bigg\}.

By (the proof of) Kiehl’s finiteness theorem, this implies that
\mathrm{wt}_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta}})
is finite over
\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}
. Yet,
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta}
is flat over
\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}
by
[
Bu07
, Lemma 4.1]
and
\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}
is irreducible.
So
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta}
has constant degree over
\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon),\delta}
. Letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
(while
\epsilon_{\delta}\to 0^{+}
), we deduce that
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}=\bigcup_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}^{\delta}
is finite and flat of constant degree over
\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}
.

It remains to compute this degree for each
n
. We have proved in Proposition
4.1
(2) that for each
k
such that
n=d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\varepsilon\cdot(1\times\omega^{2-k}))
,
\big(n,v_{p}(g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{k}))\big)
is a vertex of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-)\big)
; in particular,
w_{k}\in\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}
. In this case, §
2.4
(7) (applied separately to
\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{a,b})
and to
\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket}(\sigma_{p-1-a,a+b+1})
if
\bar{r}_{p}
is split) implies that

\displaystyle\deg\big(\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})_{n}\big/\mathrm{Vtx}_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}\big)=\rank_{\mathcal{O}}\mathrm{S}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\varepsilon\cdot(1\times\omega^{2-k}))

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\begin{cases}m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot n&\textrm{ when }\bar{r}_{p}\textrm{ is non-split},\\
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot n&\textrm{ when }\bar{r}_{p}\textrm{ is split and }\varepsilon\notin\{\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b},\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}\},\\
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot(n-1)+m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})&\textrm{ when }\bar{r}_{p}\textrm{ is split and }\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b},\\
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot n+m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})&\textrm{ when }\bar{r}_{p}\textrm{ is split and }\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}.\end{cases}

Here we implicitly used Proposition
2.15
to identify the ghost series for
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and for
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
. In particular, the first slope of
\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star},-))
is zero if
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
and is nonzero if
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
; hence the slight variant description above.
We also point out that when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
, applying the same argument above using
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
in places of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, we deduce that the slope zero part of
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
has degree
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
over
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
.

From this, we immediately deduce the slopes of
\NP\big(C^{(\varepsilon)}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
at each point
w_{\star}\in\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
are exactly
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
disjoint copies of the multiset of the slopes of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, except that the slope zero part of
\NP\big(C^{(\varepsilon)}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}(w_{\star},-)\big)

•

has length
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b+1}
, and

•

has length
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
.

Theorem
8.7
is proved.
∎

Remark 8.8
.

(1) The construction of the spectral curve in Step I using Buzzard’s eigenvariety machine in Step I agrees with Emerton’s construction, as explained in the proof of
[
Em06
, Proposition 4.2.36]
.

(2) We expect that our method of proof can be generalized to the case of
\bar{r}
-localized space of modular forms when the global residual Galois representation
\bar{r}
is reducible.
In this case, the corresponding
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
is no longer projective as an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-module, causing some trouble. We leave this to interested readers.

In what follows, we give three applications: Gouvêa–Mazur conjecture, Gouvêa’s distribution conjecture, and a refined spectral halo theorem. We refer to §
1.17
, §
1.20
, and §
1.23
, respectively, for a discussion on the history of these conjectures. Here, we give directly their statements and proofs.
These applications share the following setup.

Notation 8.9
.

For the rest of this section, assume that
p\geq 11
.
Let
\bar{r}_{p}
be a residual Galois representation as in Notation
7.2
with
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
as therein. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
be an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}
and multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
.

Fix a character
\varepsilon
of
\Delta^{2}
relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, let

(8.9.1)

\alpha_{1}^{(\varepsilon)}(k),\alpha_{2}^{(\varepsilon)}(k),\dots

denote the list of
U_{p}
-slopes on
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\dagger,(\varepsilon)}
counted with multiplicity, which contains the
U_{p}
-slopes on
\mathrm{S}_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\varepsilon\cdot(1\times\omega^{2-k}))
as the first
d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\varepsilon\cdot(1\times\omega^{2-k}))
terms.

Theorem 8.10

(
\bar{r}_{p}
-version of Gouvêa–Mazur conjecture)
.

Keep the notation and assumptions in Notation
8.9
. Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 4}
. For weights
k_{1},k_{2}\geq m-2
such that
v_{p}(k_{1}-k_{2})\geq m
, the sequence of
U_{p}
-slopes (
8.9.1
) for
k_{1}
and for
k_{2}
agree up to slope
m-4
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.7
, the sequence (
8.9.1
) (except for possibly the first several zeros) is precisely the slopes of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-)\big)
with multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
. This then follows from
[
Re

24

, Theorem 1.4]
, which proved the corresponding statement for the ghost slopes.
∎

Theorem 8.11

(
\bar{r}_{p}
-version of Gouvêa’s slope distribution conjecture)
.

Keep the notations and assumptions in Notation
8.9
.
For each
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
, write
\mu_{k}
denote the uniform probability measure for the multiset

\bigg\{\frac{\alpha_{1}^{(\varepsilon)}(k)}{k-1},\ \frac{\alpha_{2}^{(\varepsilon)}(k)}{k-1},\ \dots,\ \frac{\alpha_{d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})}^{(\varepsilon)}(k)}{k-1}\bigg\}\subset[0,1].

(1)

We have the dimension formula

d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})

d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})

\bar{r}_{p}
split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})-m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-2m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})

\bar{r}_{p}
split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})+m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})+2m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})

otherwise

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})

m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cdot d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})

We have the following estimates:

\alpha_{i}(k)=\begin{cases}\frac{p-1}{2}\cdot\frac{i}{m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}+O(\log k)&\textrm{ when $1\leq i\leq d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})$},\\
\frac{k-2}{2}&\textrm{ when $d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})<i\leq d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})$},\\
\frac{p-1}{2}\cdot\frac{i}{m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}+O(\log k)&\textrm{ when $d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})<i\leq d_{k,\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})$}.\end{cases}

(2)

As
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
with
k_{\bullet}\to\infty
, the measure
\mu_{k}
weakly converges to the probability measure

\frac{1}{p+1}\delta_{[0,\frac{1}{p+1}]}+\frac{1}{p+1}\delta_{[\frac{p}{p+1},1]}+\frac{p-1}{p+1}\delta_{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\delta_{[a,b]}
denotes the uniform probability measure on the interval
[a,b]
, and
\delta_{\frac{1}{2}}
is the Dirac measure at
\frac{1}{2}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.7
, the sequence (
8.9.1
) is precisely the slopes of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-)\big)
with multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
(except when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
or
\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
, the multiplicity of the slope zero part are precisely
m^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathrm{H}})
and
m^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mathrm{H}})
, respectively). The power series
G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w,t)
is an abstract ghost series in the sense of
[
BP19a
]
with

A=\frac{2m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}{p+1}\quad\textrm{and}\quad B=\frac{2(p-1)\cdot m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})}{p+1}

by Definition-Proposition
2.13
(and §
2.4
(7)). With this, the theorem follow from
[
BP19b
, Theorem 3.1 and Corollary 3.2]
.
∎

Theorem 8.12

(Refined spectral halo conjecture)
.

Keep the notations and assumptions in Notation
8.9
. Let
\mathrm{wt}:\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}\to\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
be the projection to weight space, and let
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
denote the zero locus of
C^{(\varepsilon)}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}(w,t)
in
\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rig}}
. Set

\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}=\big\{w_{\star}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}\;\big|\;v_{p}(w_{\star})\in(0,1)\big\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\cap\mathrm{wt}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}).

Then
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is a disjoint union
Y_{0}\bigsqcup Y_{1}\bigsqcup Y_{2}\bigsqcup\cdots
such that

(1)

Y_{0}
is non-empty only when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
, in which case, for each point
(w_{\star},a_{p})\in Y_{0}
,
v_{p}(a_{p})=0
, and
\deg\big(Y_{0}/\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}\big)=m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
.

(2)

for each point
(w_{\star},a_{p})\in Y_{n}
with
n\geq 1
,
v_{p}(a_{p})=(\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{n-1}^{(\varepsilon)})\cdot v_{p}(w_{\star})
, and

(3)

the weight map
\mathrm{wt}:Y_{n}\to\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}
is finite and flat of degree
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
, except when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split,
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
, and
n=1
, in which case
\deg\big(Y_{1}/\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}\big)=m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.7
, the sequence (
8.9.1
) is precisely the slopes of
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w_{k},-)\big)
with multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
(except when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
or
\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
, the multiplicity of the slope zero part are precisely
m^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathrm{H}})
and
m^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mathrm{H}})
, respectively).
But when
v_{p}(w_{\star})\in(0,1)
, we have
v_{p}(g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}(w_{\star}))=\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}\cdot v_{p}(w_{\star})
. Moreover, Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4) says that the differences
\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}-\deg g_{n-1}^{(\varepsilon)}
is strictly increasing in
n
. It follows that we may “distribute” the points
(w_{\star},a_{p})\in\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{(0,1)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
by the ratio
v_{p}(a_{p})/v_{p}(w_{\star})
into the disjoint spaces
Y_{n}
as described in (1) and (2). The theorem is clear.
∎

9.
Irreducible components of eigencurves

In this section, we prove the finiteness of irreducible components of the spectral curve associated to an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
of type
\bar{r}_{p}
.
In particular, this applies to the case of eigencurves associated to overconvergent modular forms (with appropriate Hecke maximal ideal localization) and provides some positive theoretical evidence towards a question asked by Coleman and Mazur in their seminal paper
[
CM98
, page 4]
, under our reducible nonsplit and very generic condition.

We will separate the discussion for the ordinary part and the non-ordinary part.

Notation 9.1
.

Let
\bar{r}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{1})\omega_{1}^{a+b+1}&*\\
0&\mathrm{unr}(\bar{\alpha}_{2})\omega_{1}^{b}\end{pmatrix}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}=\sigma_{a,b}=\Sym^{a}\mathbb{F}^{\oplus 2}\otimes\mathrm{det}^{b}
be as in Notation
7.2
and let
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
be an
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathrm{K}_{p}\rrbracket
-projective arithmetic module of type
\bar{r}_{p}
and multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
as defined in Definition
8.2
.

For a character
\varepsilon
of
\Delta^{2}
relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, define the non-ordinary part of the ghost series to be

G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(w,t):=\begin{cases}\big(G^{(\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b})}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w,t)-1\big)/t&\textrm{ if }\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b},\\
G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(w,t)&\textrm{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that Definition-Proposition
2.13
(4) says that
\deg g_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}=0
only happens when
n=1
and
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
. By Proposition
2.15
(4), for
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}=\sigma_{p-3-a,a+b+1}
, we have an equality of power series
G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\mathrm{nord}}(w,t)=G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(w,t)
.

The following is the main subject of our study.

Definition 9.2
.

Fix a rational number
\lambda\in(0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
. Put
\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}:=\Spm E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle
. Recall from Notation
2.25
that a Fredholm series over
\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}
is a power series
F(w,t)\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle\llbracket t\rrbracket
such that
f(w,0)=1
and
F(w,t)
converges over
\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{rig}}
. We say
F
is nontrivial if
F\neq 1
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{Z}(F)
denote its zero in
\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{rig}}
, as a rigid analytic subvariety.

(2)

We say
F(w,t)
is
of ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
, if for every
w_{\star}\in\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
,
\NP(F(w_{\star},-))
is the same as
\NP\big(G^{(\varepsilon)}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(w_{\star},-)\big)
, but stretched in the
x
- and
y
-directions by some
m(F)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. This
m(F)
is called the
multiplicity
of
F
. We also call the subvariety
\mathcal{Z}(F)
of
ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
.
(In fact, any power series
F(w,t)=1+f_{1}(w)t+\cdots\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle\llbracket t\rrbracket
satisfying the same Newton polygon condition for ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
is automatically a Fredholm series.)

We emphasize that the condition
\lambda\in(0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
implies that
\mathcal{W}_{\geq\lambda}
contains some “halo region”, namely some part that Theorem
8.12
applies (even though our argument does not use Theorem
8.12
logically).

The following lemma factors out the slope zero part of the characteristic power series.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\bar{r}_{p}
,
\varepsilon
, and
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
be as in Notation
9.1
with
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
and
p\geq 11
. Let
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)=1+\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}c_{n}^{(\varepsilon)}(w)t^{n}\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket w,t\rrbracket
denote the characteristic power series of
U_{p}
-action on the abstract overconvergent forms associated to
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
. Then there is a factorization in
\mathcal{O}\llbracket w,t\rrbracket
:

(9.3.1)

C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)=C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)\cdot C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t),

such that
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
is a Fredholm series of ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
with multiplicity
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
and
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
is a polynomial

•

of degree
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
and
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit,

•

of degree
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
and
\bar{r}_{p}
is split,

•

of degree
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
when
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
and
\bar{r}_{p}
is split, and

•

of degree
0
otherwise.

Moreover, the constant term of
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
is
1
and the top degree coefficient of
C_{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,t)
belongs to
\mathcal{O}\llbracket w\rrbracket^{\times}
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
8.7
and the Weierstrass Preparation Theorem.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

In fact, Lemma
9.3
holds under a weaker assumption such as
1\leq a\leq p-4
and
p\geq 5
.

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
F(w,t)\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle\llbracket t\rrbracket
be a nontrivial Fredholm series. Then there exists a unique nonempty set of positive integers
\{n_{i}\}
and nonempty finite set of distinct irreducible nontrivial Fredholm series
\{P_{i}\}
such that
F=\prod P_{i}^{n_{i}}
. Moreover, the irreducible components of
\mathcal{Z}(F)
endowed with their reduced structures are the
\mathcal{Z}(P_{i})
’s.

Proof.

This is
[
CM98
, Theorem 1.3.7]
and
[
Con99
, Corollary 4.2.3]
.
∎

The main theorem of this section is the following (which holds under the weaker conditions
p\geq 5
and
1\leq a\leq p-4
).

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
F(w,t)\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle\llbracket t\rrbracket
be a nontrivial Fredholm series of ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
with multiplicity
m(F)
. Then any Fredholm series
H(w,t)
dividing
F(w,t)
is of ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
with some multiplicity
m(H)\leq m(F)
.

The proof of Theorem
9.6
will occupy the rest of this section. We note the following.

Corollary 9.7
.

Let
\bar{r}_{p}
,
\varepsilon
, and
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}
be as in Lemma
9.3
, and in particular
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
and
p\geq 11
. Then
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\bigsqcup\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{nord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is a disjoint union of the slope zero subspace and the positive slope subspace.

(1)

The ordinary subspace
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is nonempty only when
\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}
, or when
\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}
and
\bar{r}_{p}
is split; in this case,
\mathrm{wt}:\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\to\mathcal{W}^{(\varepsilon)}
is finite and flat; its degree is

\begin{cases}m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}),&\textrm{if $\bar{r}_{p}$ is nonsplit and $\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}$},\\
m^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}),&\textrm{if $\bar{r}_{p}$ is split and $\varepsilon=\omega^{b}\times\omega^{a+b}$},\\
m^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}),&\textrm{if $\bar{r}_{p}$ is split and $\varepsilon=\omega^{a+b+1}\times\omega^{b-1}$}.\end{cases}

(2)

The non-ordinary subspace
\Spc_{\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
has finitely many irreducible components and every irreducible component is of ghost type
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon
, and the total multiplicity is
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
. In particular, if
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=1
,
\Spc_{\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is irreducible.

Proof.

The factorization in Lemma
9.3
gives the decomposition
\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})\bigsqcup\Spc^{(\varepsilon)}_{\mathrm{nord}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
, and (2) follows from Theorem
9.6
immediately.
∎

Further specializing Corollary
9.7
to the case of modular forms proves Theorem
1.16
.

Remark 9.8
.

(1)

While Theorem
9.6
works for
a\in\{1,\dots,p-4\}
, Corollary
9.7
holds under the slightly more restrictive assumption that
a\in\{2,\dots,p-5\}
and
p\geq 11
, which is needed because of Theorem
8.7
.

(2)

A philosophical implication of Theorem
9.6
and Corollary
9.7
is that
the non-ordinary part of the spectral curve shares certain “rigidity” or “finiteness” similar to that of the ordinary part.

(3)

It is clear from Corollary
9.7
that if
\bar{r}_{p}
is nonsplit and
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=1
, then
\Spc_{\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is irreducible. It is natural to ask: when
\bar{r}_{p}
is split and
m(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})=2
, can one prove that
\Spc_{\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is irreducible?

In general, suppose that we are in an automorphic setting with all tame local conditions being “primitive” (e.g. having
\ell
-adic Breuil–Mézard multiplicity one), does it imply that
\Spc_{\mathrm{nord}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is irreducible?

Notation 9.9
.

Fix
\lambda\in(0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
for the rest of this section.

For a rigid analytic space
Z
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, write
\overline{Z}
for the base change to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, and
\overline{Z}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
for the Berkovich space associated to
\overline{Z}
.
For a closed point
w_{\star}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}
and
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
write the closed disk of radius
p^{-r}
centered at
w_{\star}
as

\mathbf{D}(w_{\star},r):=\big\{w\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbb{C}_{p})\;\big|\;v_{p}(w-w_{\star})\geq r\big\}.

In what follows, it will be technically more convenient to make use of Berkovich spaces. For a closed point
w_{\star}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}
and
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, write
\eta_{w_{\star},r}
to denote the Gaussian point associated to the disk
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{\star},r)
on
\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
.

We also recall from Notation
2.25
the Newton polygons at Berkovich points, the continuity of Newton polygon as the Berkovich points vary (Lemma
2.26
), and the Berkovich subspace of
\overline{\mathcal{W}}
where
x=n
is a vertex of
\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\varepsilon)}(w,-))
(Corollary
2.27
).

The following standard harmonicity fact is key to our proof of Theorem
9.6
; see for example
[
Ke10
, Proposition 11.1.2]
.

Definition-Lemma 9.10
.

Use
\breve{\mathcal{O}}
to denote the completion of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathcal{O}
with fraction field
\breve{E}
and residual field
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
.
Let
f(w)\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle
be a power series,
w_{\star}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
a closed point, and
\mu\in(\lambda,\infty)\cap\mathbb{Z}
.
Define the following slope derivatives: for
\bar{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}
(fixing a lift
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}
of
\bar{\alpha}
)

(9.10.1)

\begin{split}V_{w_{\star},\mu}^{+}(f):=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\epsilon^{-1}\cdot\big(v_{p}\big(f(\eta_{w_{\star},\mu-\epsilon})\big)-v_{p}\big(f(\eta_{w_{\star},\mu})\big)\big),\quad\\
V_{w_{\star},\mu}^{\bar{\alpha}}(f):=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\epsilon^{-1}\cdot\big(v_{p}\big(f(\eta_{w_{\star}+\alpha p^{\mu},\mu+\epsilon})\big)-v_{p}\big(f(\eta_{w_{\star},\mu})\big)\big).\end{split}

In other words,
V^{+}_{w_{\star},\mu}
(resp.
V^{\bar{\alpha}}_{w_{\star},\mu}
) measures the rate of change of the
p
-adic valuations of
f
when we move from the Gaussian point
\eta_{w_{\star},\mu}
towards a larger radius (resp. towards a smaller radius in the disk centered at
w_{\star}+\alpha p^{\mu}
.) Each of
V^{\bar{\alpha}}_{w_{\star},\mu}(f)
does not depend on the choice of the lift
\alpha
, and, for fixed
f
,
w_{\star}
, and
\mu
, there are only finitely many nonzero
V^{\bar{\alpha}}_{w_{\star},\mu}(f)
’s.

Then we have

(9.10.2)

V_{w_{\star},\mu}^{+}(f)+\sum_{\bar{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}}V_{w_{\star},\mu}^{\bar{\alpha}}(f)=0.

Such definition and harmonicity (
9.10.2
) extends in a natural way to rational functions of the form
f(w)/g(w)
with
f(w),g(w)\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle
by setting
V^{?}_{w_{\star},\mu}(f/g):=V^{?}_{w_{\star},\mu}(f)-V^{?}_{w_{\star},\mu}(g)
with
?=+
or
\bar{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}
(whenever the limits exist).

9.11.
Proof of Theorem
9.6

In this entire proof, we fix a character
\varepsilon
relevant to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
and suppress all superscripts
(\varepsilon)
.
Assume that
F(w,t)=H(w,t)\cdot H^{\prime}(w,t)
for Fredholm series
H,H^{\prime}\in E\langle w/p^{\lambda}\rangle\llbracket t\rrbracket
.
Then for any Berkovich point
\mathsf{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
, the slopes in
\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))
(resp.
\NP(H^{\prime}(\mathsf{w},-))
) form a subset of slopes of
\NP(F(\mathsf{w},-))
, which is the same as the set of slopes of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
.
Put

F(w,t)=1+f_{1}(w)t+\cdots,\ H(w,t)=1+h_{1}(w)t+\cdots,\ \textrm{and}\ H^{\prime}(w,t)=1+h^{\prime}_{1}(w)t+\cdots.

Recall from Corollary
2.27
that for each fixed
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, all elements
\mathsf{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
for which
(n,v_{p}(g_{n}(\mathsf{w})))
is a vertex of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
form a Berkovich subspace:

\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}:=\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{k}\overline{\mathbf{D}}\big(w_{k},\,\Delta_{k,|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-n|+1}-\Delta_{k,|\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-n|}\big)^{\mathrm{Berk}},

where the union is taken over all
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k_{\bullet}
such that
n\in\big(d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1}),d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})-d_{k}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\varepsilon_{1})\big)
. The Berkovich space
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n,\geq\lambda}
is clearly connected.

In what follows, we write
\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w})
for the
n
th slope in
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
.
The proof is divided into three steps.

Step I
: For each
n
, we will prove that the total multiplicity of the
n
smallest slopes of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
in
\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))
is constant in
\mathsf{w}\in\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
; write
m(H,n)
for this constant. We define
m(H^{\prime},n)
for
H^{\prime}
similarly. It is clear that
m(H,n)+m(H^{\prime},n)=n\cdot m(F)
.

It suffices to show that the total multiplicity
\mathrm{totmult}_{n}(\mathsf{w})
of those slopes in
\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))
that are less than or equal to
\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w})
, is a locally constant function on
\mathsf{w}\in\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n,\geq\lambda}
. We proceed by induction on
n
and start from the trivial case
n=0
. Now suppose that the claim is proved for smaller
n
’s. For
\mathsf{w}\in\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
, suppose
\mathrm{totmult}_{n}(\mathsf{w})=m
, which is obviously less than or equal to
n\cdot m(F)
. Since
(n,v_{p}(g_{n}(\mathsf{w})))
is a vertex of
\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
, the slope difference
\mu:=\mathrm{slp}_{n+1}(\mathsf{w})-\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w})>0
. On the other hand,
\mathsf{w}\mapsto\NP\big(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
and
\mathsf{w}\mapsto\NP\big(H(\mathsf{w},-)\big)
are continuous for the Berkovich topology by Lemma
2.26
(1). We may choose an open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathsf{w}
in
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n,\geq\lambda}
such that for every
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
, we have

\big|\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=i}-\NP(H(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=i}\big|<\tfrac{\mu}{4}\textrm{\hskip 11.49994ptfor }i=m-1,m,m+1,\textrm{ and}

\big|\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=j}-\NP(G_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathrm{nord}}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=j}\big|<\tfrac{\mu}{4}\textrm{\hskip 11.49994ptfor }j=n-1,n,n+1.

Then we have

\mathrm{slp}_{n+1}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})>\mathrm{slp}_{n+1}(\mathsf{w})-\tfrac{\mu}{2}>\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w})+\tfrac{\mu}{2}>\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}),\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle\big|\NP(H(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=m}-\NP(H(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=m-1}\big|

\displaystyle<\

\displaystyle\big|\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=m}-\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=m-1}\big|+\tfrac{\mu}{4}\cdot 2=\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w})+\tfrac{\mu}{2}<\mathrm{slp}_{n+1}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}).

\displaystyle\big|\NP(H(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=m+1}-\NP(H(\mathsf{w}^{\prime},-))_{x=m}\big|

\displaystyle>\

\displaystyle\big|\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=m+1}-\NP(H(\mathsf{w},-))_{x=m}\big|-\tfrac{\mu}{4}\cdot 2=\mathrm{slp}_{n+1}(\mathsf{w})-\tfrac{\mu}{2}>\mathrm{slp}_{n}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}).

From this, we deduce that
\mathrm{totmult}_{n}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})=m
for every
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
. Yet
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
is connected; so
\mathrm{totmult}_{n}(-)
is constant.

Step II
: The following claim is key to our proof; it should be straightforward, but some work is needed to rule out pathological cases. For each integer
n\geq 1
, Definition-Proposition
2.13
(2) implies that there is a unique weight
k=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)(n+\delta_{\varepsilon}-1)
such that
k\equiv k_{\varepsilon}\bmod(p-1)
and
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})=n
.

Claim: for every
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and every
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
,

(1)

the point
\eta_{w_{k},\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}-\epsilon}
belongs to the subspaces
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
and
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n+1,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
of
\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}
,

(2)

the point
\eta_{w_{k}+\alpha p^{\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}},\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}+\epsilon}
does not belong to the subspaces
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
, and

(3)

the point
\eta_{w_{k}+\alpha p^{\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}},\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}+\epsilon}
belongs to the subspaces
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n+1,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
and
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n-1,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
.

Proof:
By Proposition
2.20
(3), one of the disks removed to get
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n,\geq\lambda}
is
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{k},\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0})^{\mathrm{Berk}}
, so (2) is proved. Moreover, the point
\eta_{w_{k},\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}-\epsilon}
is not removed for this disk when considered for whether it belongs to
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n,\geq\lambda}
.

Similarly, to get
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}_{n\pm 1,\geq\lambda}^{\mathrm{Berk}}
, we need to remove the disk
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{k},\Delta_{k,2}-\Delta_{k,1})^{\mathrm{Berk}}
. But by
[
LTXZ

24

, Lemmas 5.6 and 5.8]
, we have
\Delta_{k,2}-\Delta_{k,1}\geq\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}+1
; so none of the points in (1) and (3) belong to this disk
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{k},\Delta_{k,2}-\Delta_{k,1})^{\mathrm{Berk}}
.

It then suffices to explain that the points in (1) and (3) are not contained in any other disks removed to get
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n-s,\geq\lambda}
with
s\in\{\pm 1,0\}
.

Now, take any
k^{\prime}=k_{\varepsilon}+(p-1)k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\neq k
and any
s\in\{\pm 1,0\}
. The condition
\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})=n
can be rewritten (via Definition-Proposition
2.13
) as

(n-s)-\tfrac{1}{2}d_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})=k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s.

By Proposition
2.20
(3), the corresponding disk removed from
\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\geq\lambda}
to get
\overline{\mathrm{Vtx}}^{\mathrm{Berk}}_{n-s,\geq\lambda}
is precisely
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{k^{\prime}},\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|})^{\mathrm{Berk}}
.

Suppose for contrary that
\overline{\mathbf{D}}(w_{k^{\prime}},\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|})^{\mathrm{Berk}}
contains one of the points in (1) and (3) for some
s\in\{\pm 1,0\}
.
Then we have

•

(for the radii)
\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}+\epsilon\geq\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|}
, and

•

(for the centers)
v_{p}(w_{k^{\prime}}-w_{k})\geq\min\big\{\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|},\ \Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}-\epsilon\big\}
.

Yet the differences
\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|}
and
\Delta_{k,1}-\Delta_{k,0}
belong to
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
by Proposition
2.20
(6), and
v_{p}(w_{k^{\prime}}-w_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}
. The condition
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
guarantees that the two inequalities above still hold after setting
\epsilon=0
by integrality. In particular,

(9.11.1)

v_{p}(w_{k^{\prime}}-w_{k})\geq\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|+1}-\Delta_{k^{\prime},|k_{\bullet}-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-s|}.

This inequality implies that
n-s\in\overline{\mathrm{nS}}_{w_{k^{\prime}},k}
by Definition
2.19
, and thus
\mathrm{nS}_{w_{k^{\prime}},k}
contains at least one of
\{n-2,n-1,\dots,n+2\}
. This would imply by Proposition
2.20
(5) that at least one of
(0,\Delta_{k,0})
,
(1,\Delta_{k,1})
, or
(2,\Delta_{k,2})
is not a vertex of
\underline{\Delta}_{k}
